<a id='question-129'></a>

### Question 129: Transplantation: Part 3 Focus 29 (Allograft)
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Understanding the immunological mechanisms underlying acute cellular rejection of an allograft, specifically the role of T lymphocytes (CD8+ cytotoxic T cells) and their interaction with graft cells, leading to graft damage. (理解同基因移植片急性细胞性排斥的免疫机制，特别是T淋巴细胞（CD8+细胞毒性T细胞）的作用及其与移植细胞的相互作用导致移植片损伤。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 52-year-old male with end-stage renal disease due to diabetic nephropathy receives a kidney allograft from a deceased donor with one HLA mismatch at the A locus. He is started on standard immunosuppressive therapy consisting of tacrolimus, mycophenolate mofetil, and prednisone. Three weeks post-transplant, he develops fever, flank pain, decreased urine output, and elevated serum creatinine. A renal biopsy reveals diffuse interstitial inflammation with lymphocytic infiltrates, tubular epithelial cell injury, and evidence of endothelialitis. Immunofluorescence staining shows C4d deposition in peritubular capillaries. Flow cytometry analysis of the peripheral blood shows a significant increase in CD8+ T cells.

Which of the following cellular mechanisms is MOST likely responsible for the observed graft injury?

(A) Antibody-dependent cell-mediated cytotoxicity (ADCC) mediated by NK cells recognizing graft cells coated with donor-specific antibodies.
(B) Complement-dependent cytotoxicity (CDC) mediated by antibodies binding to donor HLA antigens on graft endothelial cells.
(C) Direct cytotoxicity mediated by CD8+ T lymphocytes recognizing donor HLA class I antigens presented on graft cells.
(D) Macrophage-mediated phagocytosis of graft cells opsonized by antibodies and complement.
(E) Type III hypersensitivity reaction involving the deposition of immune complexes in the glomeruli.

#### Correct Answer: C

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical/Microbiological Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Profile:** 52-year-old male, end-stage renal disease (ESRD) from diabetes, received kidney allograft. This sets the stage for transplantation immunology.
- **Graft Type:** Kidney allograft (from deceased donor, one HLA mismatch). This indicates potential for immune rejection.
- **Immunosuppression:** Tacrolimus (calcineurin inhibitor, inhibits T cell activation), mycophenolate mofetil (inhibits purine synthesis, affects lymphocyte proliferation), prednisone (corticosteroid, broad immunosuppressant). Despite this, rejection occurs.
- **Timing:** Three weeks post-transplant. This timeframe is typical for acute rejection, particularly T cell-mediated rejection (TCMR).
- **Symptoms:** Fever, flank pain, decreased urine output, elevated creatinine. These are classic signs of acute graft dysfunction, suggestive of rejection.
- **Biopsy Findings:** Diffuse interstitial inflammation with lymphocytic infiltrates, tubular epithelial cell injury, endothelialitis. This points towards cellular rejection. Endothelialitis (inflammation of endothelial cells) is a hallmark of acute T-cell mediated rejection (TCMR).
- **Immunofluorescence:** C4d deposition in peritubular capillaries. C4d is a stable breakdown product of complement component C4b. Its presence indicates recent complement activation, often associated with antibody-mediated rejection (AMR), but can also be seen in TCMR, particularly in cases with mixed rejection or endothelial injury triggering complement activation. However, the predominant histological picture (lymphocytic infiltrates, tubular injury, endothelialitis) strongly favors TCMR.
- **Flow Cytometry:** Increased CD8+ T cells in peripheral blood. CD8+ T cells are cytotoxic T lymphocytes (CTLs) and are the primary effector cells in direct cellular rejection.
- **Question Stem:** Asks for the MOST likely cellular mechanism responsible for graft injury. Given the clinical picture, biopsy findings (especially lymphocytic infiltrates and endothelialitis), and increased CD8+ T cells, the mechanism involves direct T cell cytotoxicity.

##### 2. Immunological, Pathological & Virulence Deep-Dive (免疫机制、微生物致病机理与分子生物学深度剖析)
- **Acute Cellular Rejection (ACR):** Primarily mediated by T lymphocytes (CD4+ helper T cells and CD8+ cytotoxic T cells) recognizing donor HLA antigens presented on graft cells.
- **Mechanism of CD8+ T cell cytotoxicity:** CD8+ T cells recognize foreign MHC class I molecules (donor HLA class I) presenting peptides on the surface of graft cells (e.g., tubular epithelial cells, endothelial cells). This interaction, along with co-stimulatory signals, activates the CD8+ T cell. Activated CD8+ T cells then directly kill the target graft cells through several mechanisms:
    - **Perforin/Granzyme Pathway:** CD8+ T cells release perforin, which forms pores in the target cell membrane, and granzymes, which enter the cell through these pores and activate caspases, leading to apoptosis.
    - **Fas/FasL Pathway:** CD8+ T cells express Fas ligand (FasL), which binds to Fas (CD95) receptors on target cells, triggering the extrinsic apoptotic pathway.
- **Role of CD4+ T cells:** CD4+ T cells recognize donor HLA class II molecules and can help activate CD8+ T cells and macrophages, contributing to the inflammatory response.
- **Antibody-Mediated Rejection (AMR):** Mediated by antibodies (donor-specific antibodies, DSAs) against donor HLA antigens. AMR can cause graft injury through several mechanisms:
    - **Complement-Dependent Cytotoxicity (CDC):** Antibodies bind to donor HLA antigens on endothelial cells, activating the classical complement pathway, leading to cell lysis.
    - **Antibody-Dependent Cell-Mediated Cytotoxicity (ADCC):** Antibodies coat graft cells, which are then recognized and killed by effector cells like NK cells or macrophages expressing Fc receptors.
    - **Inflammation and Endothelial Injury:** Antibody binding and complement activation cause endothelial cell activation, inflammation, and thrombosis.
- **C4d Deposition:** Indicates complement activation. While classically associated with AMR, it can be present in TCMR, especially with endothelialitis, as T cell-mediated endothelial damage can trigger complement activation. However, the dominant histological features here (lymphocytic infiltrates, tubular injury) point strongly towards TCMR.
- **Flow Cytometry:** Increased CD8+ T cells support the role of cellular cytotoxicity in the rejection process.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A) **Antibody-dependent cell-mediated cytotoxicity (ADCC) mediated by NK cells recognizing graft cells coated with donor-specific antibodies.** This describes a mechanism of Antibody-Mediated Rejection (AMR), not the primary mechanism in this case. While C4d deposition can be seen in AMR, the predominant histological findings (lymphocytic infiltrates, tubular injury, endothelialitis) and the increase in CD8+ T cells strongly favor TCMR. ADCC involves NK cells or macrophages, not primarily CD8+ T cells directly killing graft cells.
- (B) **Complement-dependent cytotoxicity (CDC) mediated by antibodies binding to donor HLA antigens on graft endothelial cells.** This is another mechanism of AMR. It involves antibodies activating complement, leading to cell lysis. Again, the histological picture and increased CD8+ T cells are more consistent with TCMR. CDC is primarily an antibody-mediated process, whereas the vignette points towards a T cell-mediated process.
- (D) **Macrophage-mediated phagocytosis of graft cells opsonized by antibodies and complement.** This is also a mechanism associated with AMR, where antibodies and complement components (like C3b) opsonize graft cells, making them targets for phagocytosis by macrophages. This is not the primary mechanism described by the clinical presentation and biopsy findings suggestive of direct T cell attack.
- (E) **Type III hypersensitivity reaction involving the deposition of immune complexes in the glomeruli.** This involves the deposition of circulating antigen-antibody complexes in tissues, leading to inflammation. While immune complexes can form in transplant recipients, this mechanism typically affects glomeruli (causing glomerulonephritis) and is not the primary cause of the diffuse interstitial inflammation, tubular injury, and endothelialitis seen in this biopsy, which are characteristic of acute cellular rejection.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点与易混淆病原体/免疫缺陷横向对比):
| Feature                 | Acute Cellular Rejection (TCMR) | Antibody-Mediated Rejection (AMR) | Type III Hypersensitivity |
|--------------------------|---------------------------------|-----------------------------------|---------------------------|
| **Primary Mediators**    | T lymphocytes (CD8+, CD4+)      | Antibodies (DSAs)                 | Immune Complexes         |
| **Key Histology**        | Lymphocytic infiltrates, tubular injury, endothelialitis | Endothelialitis, C4d deposition, microvascular inflammation | Glomerular deposits, inflammation |
| **Mechanism**            | Direct cytotoxicity, cytokine release | CDC, ADCC, inflammation         | Immune complex deposition |
| **C4d Deposition**       | Possible, especially with endothelialitis | Characteristic                  | Not typical               |
| **CD8+ T cell increase** | Characteristic                  | Not primary feature              | Not relevant              |
| **Example**              | This case                       | DSA-positive rejection            | Post-transplant glomerulonephritis |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
Acute cellular rejection is primarily mediated by T lymphocytes, particularly CD8+ cytotoxic T cells, directly killing graft cells. Histological hallmarks include lymphocytic infiltrates, tubular injury, and endothelialitis. Increased peripheral CD8+ T cells support this diagnosis. Differentiate TCMR from AMR, which is mediated by antibodies and involves mechanisms like CDC, ADCC, and complement activation (C4d deposition). Type III hypersensitivity involves immune complex deposition, typically in glomeruli.

<a id='question-130'></a>

### Question 130: Transplantation: Part 3 Focus 30 (rejection)
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Understanding the cellular and molecular mechanisms underlying acute cellular rejection in organ transplantation, specifically the role of T lymphocytes (CD8+ cytotoxic T cells) and their effector mechanisms against allogeneic graft cells. (理解器官移植中急性细胞性排斥的细胞和分子机制，特别是T淋巴细胞（CD8+细胞毒性T细胞）的作用及其对异体移植排斥细胞的效应机制。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 52-year-old male with end-stage renal disease due to diabetic nephropathy receives a kidney transplant from a deceased donor with one HLA mismatch at the A locus. Post-transplant, he is maintained on a standard immunosuppressive regimen including tacrolimus, mycophenolate mofetil, and prednisone. Three weeks after transplantation, he develops fever, flank pain, decreased urine output, and elevated serum creatinine. A renal biopsy reveals diffuse interstitial inflammation with infiltration of mononuclear cells, tubular epithelial cell injury, and evidence of apoptosis within the tubules. Immunofluorescence staining shows deposition of C4d in peritubular capillaries. Flow cytometry analysis of the peripheral blood shows a significant increase in CD8+ T cells expressing granzyme B and perforin. Further investigation reveals that the donor kidney expresses a novel MHC class I allele that is not recognized by the recipient's inhibitory KIRs (Killer-cell Immunoglobulin-like Receptors). Which of the following is the MOST likely mechanism directly responsible for the observed graft injury?

(A) Antibody-mediated complement-dependent cytotoxicity (CDC) targeting donor endothelial cells.
(B) CD4+ T helper cell-mediated activation of macrophages leading to fibrosis.
(C) CD8+ cytotoxic T lymphocyte (CTL)-mediated killing of graft parenchymal cells.
(D) Neutrophil-mediated acute tubular necrosis (ATN) due to ischemia-reperfusion injury.
(E) NK cell-mediated lysis of donor cells via ADCC (Antibody-Dependent Cellular Cytotoxicity).

#### Correct Answer: C

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical/Microbiological Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Profile:** 52-year-old male, end-stage renal disease (ESRD) from diabetic nephropathy, kidney transplant recipient. This sets the stage for potential transplant rejection.
- **Graft Type & Mismatch:** Kidney transplant from deceased donor, one HLA-A mismatch. HLA mismatch is a primary trigger for allorecognition and rejection.
- **Immunosuppression:** Standard regimen (tacrolimus, mycophenolate, prednisone). Despite immunosuppression, rejection can still occur.
- **Clinical Presentation:** 3 weeks post-transplant, fever, flank pain, decreased urine output, elevated creatinine. These are classic signs of acute graft dysfunction, highly suggestive of acute rejection.
- **Biopsy Findings:** Diffuse interstitial inflammation, mononuclear cell infiltration, tubular epithelial cell injury, apoptosis within tubules. This points towards cellular rejection affecting the graft parenchyma.
- **Immunofluorescence:** C4d deposition in peritubular capillaries. While C4d deposition is a hallmark of antibody-mediated rejection (AMR), it can also be seen in acute cellular rejection (ACR), particularly in cases with mixed rejection or T-cell mediated endothelial injury. However, the predominant findings (tubular injury, apoptosis, mononuclear infiltrate) strongly favor ACR.
- **Flow Cytometry:** Increased CD8+ T cells expressing granzyme B and perforin. This is a key finding indicating activation of cytotoxic T lymphocytes (CTLs), which are the primary effector cells in acute cellular rejection. Granzyme B and perforin are key mediators of CTL-mediated cytotoxicity.
- **KIR/MHC Interaction:** Donor MHC class I allele not recognized by recipient's inhibitory KIRs. This lack of inhibitory signaling promotes activation of NK cells and potentially CTLs, contributing to rejection. However, the direct effector mechanism causing the observed tubular injury is CTL-mediated killing.
- **Question Stem:** Asks for the MOST likely mechanism directly responsible for the observed graft injury (tubular injury and apoptosis).

##### 2. Immunological, Pathological & Virulence Deep-Dive (免疫机制、微生物致病机理与分子生物学深度剖析)
- **Acute Cellular Rejection (ACR):** Primarily mediated by T lymphocytes recognizing donor alloantigens (HLA molecules) presented on graft cells.
    - **Sensitization Phase:** Recipient T cells (CD4+ and CD8+) recognize donor HLA antigens presented by donor antigen-presenting cells (APCs) or recipient APCs that have processed donor antigens. This leads to T cell activation and proliferation.
    - **Effector Phase:** Activated T cells migrate to the graft.
        - **CD4+ T cells:** Primarily involved in orchestrating the immune response. They can activate macrophages (leading to inflammation and fibrosis, characteristic of chronic rejection) and help activate CD8+ T cells.
        - **CD8+ T cells (CTLs):** Recognize donor MHC class I molecules presenting peptides on graft cells (parenchymal cells, endothelial cells). Upon recognition, CTLs release cytotoxic granules containing perforin and granzymes. Perforin forms pores in the target cell membrane, allowing granzymes to enter and induce apoptosis. Fas ligand (FasL) on CTLs can also bind to Fas receptor on target cells, triggering apoptosis. This direct killing of graft cells is the hallmark of acute cellular rejection, explaining the tubular injury and apoptosis seen in the biopsy.
- **Antibody-Mediated Rejection (AMR):** Mediated by antibodies (pre-formed or de novo) against donor HLA antigens or other graft antigens. Antibodies can activate complement (CDC), bind to Fc receptors on effector cells (ADCC, NK cell activation), or directly affect endothelial cell function. C4d deposition is a marker of complement activation, often seen in AMR.
- **Role of KIRs:** Killer-cell Immunoglobulin-like Receptors (KIRs) are inhibitory receptors expressed on NK cells and some T cells. They recognize specific MHC class I alleles. If the donor expresses an MHC class I allele that is not recognized by the recipient's inhibitory KIRs, the NK cells are not inhibited and can lyse the donor cells. This contributes to rejection, but the primary mechanism of *parenchymal* cell injury in this scenario, supported by the flow cytometry data (activated CD8+ CTLs with cytotoxic molecules) and biopsy findings (tubular apoptosis), is CTL-mediated killing.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A) Antibody-mediated complement-dependent cytotoxicity (CDC) targeting donor endothelial cells. While C4d deposition is present, the predominant biopsy findings (tubular injury, apoptosis, mononuclear infiltrate) and the specific flow cytometry data (activated CD8+ CTLs with granzyme B/perforin) point more strongly towards cellular rejection. CDC primarily targets endothelial cells, leading to vasculitis, which is less prominent here. AMR is a possibility, but the evidence favors ACR as the *primary* mechanism for the observed parenchymal damage.
- (B) CD4+ T helper cell-mediated activation of macrophages leading to fibrosis. CD4+ T cells are involved in ACR, but their primary role is often helper function. Macrophage activation leading to fibrosis is more characteristic of *chronic* rejection, not the acute presentation described here (3 weeks post-transplant). The biopsy shows acute tubular injury and apoptosis, not significant fibrosis.
- (D) Neutrophil-mediated acute tubular necrosis (ATN) due to ischemia-reperfusion injury. Ischemia-reperfusion injury can cause ATN, but it typically occurs immediately after transplantation during the period of ischemia and reperfusion. This patient is 3 weeks post-transplant, and the biopsy findings (mononuclear infiltrate, apoptosis) are characteristic of immune-mediated rejection, not primarily ischemic injury. Neutrophils are not the predominant cell type in the infiltrate described.
- (E) NK cell-mediated lysis of donor cells via ADCC (Antibody-Dependent Cellular Cytotoxicity). NK cells can contribute to rejection, especially when inhibitory KIR recognition is lacking. However, ADCC requires antibodies coating the target cells. While AMR can involve NK cells, the primary effector mechanism highlighted by the flow cytometry (activated CD8+ CTLs with cytotoxic molecules) and the biopsy (tubular apoptosis) is CTL-mediated killing, not NK cell-mediated ADCC.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点与易混淆病原体/免疫缺陷横向对比):
| Feature                 | Acute Cellular Rejection (ACR) | Antibody-Mediated Rejection (AMR) | Ischemia-Reperfusion Injury | Chronic Rejection |
| :---------------------- | :----------------------------- | :-------------------------------- | :-------------------------- | :---------------- |
| **Primary Mediators**   | CD8+ CTLs, CD4+ T cells        | Donor-specific antibodies         | Neutrophils, ROS            | CD4+ T cells, Fibroblasts |
| **Key Histopathology**  | Tubulitis, Parenchymal injury, Apoptosis | Endothelialitis, Glomerulopathy, C4d deposition | ATN, Brush border damage | Interstitial fibrosis, Vascular changes (graft arteriosclerosis) |
| **Typical Timing**      | Days to months post-transplant  | Days to months post-transplant     | Immediately post-transplant | Months to years   |
| **Flow Cytometry**      | Increased CD8+ CTLs (Granzyme B, Perforin) | May show B cells, plasma cells | Not specific               | Increased CD4+ T cells |
| **C4d Deposition**      | Possible, but less specific    | Hallmark                          | Absent                     | Possible          |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
Acute cellular rejection is primarily driven by CD8+ cytotoxic T lymphocytes recognizing donor MHC class I on graft cells, leading to direct cell killing via perforin/granzyme release and Fas/FasL interactions. Differentiating ACR from AMR is crucial, often relying on biopsy findings (tubulitis vs. endothelialitis), C4d deposition, and donor-specific antibody testing. Remember that flow cytometry can identify activated cytotoxic T cells as key players in ACR.

<a id='question-131'></a>

### Question 131: Transplantation: Part 3 Focus 31 (phenomena)
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Understanding the immunological mechanisms underlying graft-versus-host disease (GVHD), specifically the roles of donor T cells and recipient antigen-presenting cells (APCs) in initiating the inflammatory cascade, and the specific cytokines involved in the pathogenesis. (理解移植物抗宿主病 (GVHD) 的免疫机制，特别是供者 T 细胞和受者抗原提呈细胞 (APCs) 在启动炎症级联反应中的作用，以及参与病理发生的特定细胞因子。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 55-year-old male with a history of acute myeloid leukemia (AML) undergoes allogeneic hematopoietic stem cell transplantation (HSCT) from an HLA-matched unrelated donor. The conditioning regimen includes fludarabine and total body irradiation. Post-transplant, he develops pancytopenia, a maculopapular rash, and watery diarrhea starting 2 weeks after the transplant. Liver function tests show elevated bilirubin and alkaline phosphatase. Skin biopsy reveals apoptotic keratinocytes and lymphocytic infiltrate. Flow cytometry analysis of peripheral blood shows a significant increase in donor-derived T cells (CD3+). Further investigation reveals elevated levels of IL-1 and TNF-α in the patient's serum. Which of the following cytokines is MOST likely mediating the initial activation of recipient antigen-presenting cells (APCs) by donor T cells in this patient's graft-versus-host disease (GVHD)?

(A) IL-2
(B) IL-4
(C) IL-6
(D) IL-10
(E) IFN-γ

#### Correct Answer: (D)

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical/Microbiological Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Profile:** 55-year-old male with AML undergoing allogeneic HSCT. This sets the stage for potential GVHD.
- **Conditioning Regimen:** Fludarabine and total body irradiation. This regimen is immunosuppressive but doesn't completely eliminate the risk of GVHD, especially with T-cell containing grafts.
- **Timeline:** Symptoms (pancytopenia, rash, diarrhea, liver dysfunction) start 2 weeks post-transplant. This timing is characteristic of acute GVHD.
- **Clinical Manifestations:** Pancytopenia, maculopapular rash, watery diarrhea, elevated liver enzymes. These are classic signs of acute GVHD affecting the bone marrow, skin, gut, and liver, respectively.
- **Diagnostic Findings:**
    - Skin biopsy: Apoptotic keratinocytes and lymphocytic infiltrate confirm inflammation and tissue damage consistent with GVHD.
    - Flow cytometry: Increased donor-derived T cells (CD3+) in peripheral blood confirms engraftment of donor immune cells and their presence in the recipient's circulation.
    - Serum cytokines: Elevated IL-1 and TNF-α indicate systemic inflammation.
- **Question Stem:** Asks for the cytokine MOST likely mediating the *initial activation* of recipient APCs by donor T cells. This points to the very beginning of the GVHD cascade.

##### 2. Immunological, Pathological & Virulence Deep-Dive (免疫机制、微生物致病机理与分子生物学深度剖析)
- **GVHD Pathogenesis:** GVHD occurs when immunocompetent donor T cells (present in the graft) recognize recipient tissues as foreign (due to HLA mismatch or minor histocompatibility antigens) and mount an immune attack.
- **Initial Activation:** The process begins when donor T cells encounter recipient antigen-presenting cells (APCs), such as dendritic cells (DCs) and macrophages, in the recipient's tissues.
- **Signal 1 & 2:** Donor T cells recognize recipient MHC molecules (Signal 1) presenting recipient antigens (Signal 2).
- **Costimulation & Cytokine Release:** This interaction triggers costimulatory signals (e.g., CD28-B7) and leads to the activation of donor T cells. Activated donor T cells then release cytokines.
- **APC Activation:** Crucially, activated donor T cells release cytokines that act on recipient APCs, further activating them. This activation enhances the APCs' ability to present recipient antigens to more donor T cells, amplifying the immune response.
- **Key Cytokines in Early GVHD:**
    - **IL-1:** Produced by activated donor T cells and recipient APCs. It plays a critical role in the *initial* activation of recipient APCs, promoting their maturation and antigen-presenting capacity. It also contributes to systemic inflammation.
    - **TNF-α:** Also produced early, contributing to inflammation and tissue damage.
    - **IFN-γ:** Produced later by activated donor T cells (especially Th1 cells). It is crucial for activating macrophages and promoting cell-mediated cytotoxicity, contributing significantly to the tissue damage seen in GVHD.
    - **IL-2:** Primarily involved in T cell proliferation and differentiation.
    - **IL-4:** Associated with Th2 responses and allergic reactions; less central to the initial inflammatory phase of acute GVHD.
    - **IL-6:** A pro-inflammatory cytokine involved in various aspects of GVHD, but IL-1 is considered more pivotal for the *initial* APC activation step.
    - **IL-10:** An immunosuppressive cytokine; its role is generally to dampen the inflammatory response, not initiate it.
- **Focus on the Question:** The question specifically asks about the cytokine mediating the *initial activation* of recipient APCs. IL-1 fits this description best, acting early in the cascade to prime recipient APCs. While other cytokines like IFN-γ and TNF-α are crucial for the subsequent tissue damage and amplification of the response, IL-1 is key for the initial APC activation step triggered by donor T cells.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A) **IL-2:** Primarily functions as a T-cell growth factor, promoting the proliferation and survival of activated T cells (autocrine and paracrine effects). While essential for the expansion of the donor T cell population in GVHD, it is not the primary cytokine responsible for the *initial activation* of recipient APCs.
- (B) **IL-4:** A key cytokine for Th2 differentiation and humoral immunity. It promotes B cell class switching to IgE and is involved in allergic responses. It is not a major player in the initial inflammatory cascade of acute GVHD, which is predominantly driven by Th1-type responses.
- (C) **IL-6:** A pleiotropic pro-inflammatory cytokine involved in acute phase responses, B cell differentiation, and T cell activation. It contributes to GVHD pathogenesis, particularly in chronic GVHD and affecting specific organs like the liver. However, IL-1 is considered more critical for the *initial* activation of recipient APCs by donor T cells.
- (D) **IL-10:** An immunosuppressive cytokine produced by various immune cells, including regulatory T cells (Tregs) and macrophages. It inhibits the production of pro-inflammatory cytokines (like TNF-α, IL-1, IL-6, IL-12) and suppresses APC function. Therefore, it would *dampen*, not mediate, the initial activation of recipient APCs in GVHD.
- (E) **IFN-γ:** A hallmark cytokine of Th1 responses and a major mediator of cell-mediated immunity. It is produced by activated donor T cells (especially Th1 cells) and plays a crucial role in activating macrophages, enhancing MHC expression on target cells, and promoting cytotoxicity. While critical for the *progression* and tissue damage in GVHD, it is generally considered a downstream effector cytokine rather than the primary mediator of the *initial* APC activation by donor T cells. IL-1 acts earlier in the cascade to prime the APCs.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点与易混淆病原体/免疫缺陷横向对比):
| Cytokine | Role in Early GVHD (Initial APC Activation) | Other Key Roles | Relevance to Question |
|---|---|---|---|
| **IL-1 (Correct)** | **Primary mediator of initial recipient APC activation** | Pro-inflammation, fever, acute phase response | **MOST likely mediator of initial APC activation** |
| IL-2 | T cell proliferation, survival | T cell growth factor | Important for T cell expansion, not initial APC activation |
| IL-4 | Th2 differentiation, humoral immunity | Allergy, IgE production | Not central to acute GVHD initial phase |
| IL-6 | Pro-inflammation, acute phase response | B cell differentiation, T cell activation | Contributes to GVHD, but IL-1 is more critical for initial APC activation |
| IL-10 | Immunosuppression | Dampens inflammation | Inhibits GVHD, opposite of the question |
| IFN-γ | Macrophage activation, cell-mediated immunity | Th1 response, cytotoxicity | Crucial for GVHD tissue damage, but acts downstream of initial APC activation |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
The initial step in GVHD pathogenesis involves donor T cells activating recipient APCs. IL-1 is the key cytokine released by donor T cells that mediates this initial activation of recipient APCs, priming them to present antigens and further amplify the immune response. Understanding the sequence of cytokine release and their specific roles is crucial for differentiating the mechanisms of GVHD. Key differentials include other causes of post-transplant complications like infection or drug toxicity, but the clinical picture and flow cytometry strongly point to GVHD.

<a id='question-132'></a>

### Question 132: Transplantation: Part 3 Focus 32 (classified)
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Understanding the immunological mechanisms underlying acute cellular rejection in solid organ transplantation, specifically the role of T lymphocytes (CD8+ cytotoxic T cells) and their recognition of alloantigens presented by MHC class I molecules on graft cells. (理解实体器官移植中急性细胞性排斥的免疫机制，特别是T淋巴细胞（CD8+细胞毒性T细胞）的作用及其对移植细胞表面MHC I类分子呈递的异体抗原的识别。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 52-year-old male with end-stage renal disease due to diabetic nephropathy receives a kidney transplant from a deceased donor with two HLA mismatches at the A and B loci. He is started on standard immunosuppressive therapy consisting of tacrolimus, mycophenolate mofetil, and prednisone. Three weeks post-transplant, he develops fever, flank pain, decreased urine output, and elevated serum creatinine. A renal biopsy reveals diffuse interstitial edema, inflammatory cell infiltrates primarily composed of lymphocytes, and evidence of tubular cell injury. Immunofluorescence staining shows C4d deposition in peritubular capillaries. Flow cytometry analysis of the biopsy sample identifies a significant increase in CD8+ T cells infiltrating the graft parenchyma, particularly targeting tubular epithelial cells. Serum analysis shows normal levels of complement components C3 and C4, but elevated levels of IL-2 and IFN-γ.

Which of the following mechanisms is MOST directly responsible for the observed graft injury in this patient?

(A) Antibody-mediated rejection involving donor-specific antibodies (DSA) binding to MHC class II molecules on endothelial cells, leading to complement activation and endothelial damage.
(B) Acute cellular rejection mediated by CD4+ T helper cells recognizing donor MHC class II molecules on antigen-presenting cells (APCs) within the graft, leading to cytokine release and recruitment of other inflammatory cells.
(C) Acute cellular rejection mediated by CD8+ cytotoxic T lymphocytes (CTLs) recognizing donor MHC class I molecules on graft parenchymal cells, leading to direct cell lysis.
(D) Chronic rejection characterized by fibrosis and vascular changes, primarily driven by CD4+ T cells and fibroblasts, typically occurring months to years post-transplant.
(E) Hyperacute rejection occurring within minutes to hours post-transplant, mediated by pre-formed anti-donor antibodies against ABO blood group antigens or HLA antigens, causing immediate thrombosis and graft necrosis.

#### Correct Answer: C

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical/Microbiological Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
*   **Patient Profile:** 52-year-old male, end-stage renal disease, kidney transplant recipient. This sets the context for transplantation immunology.
*   **Transplant Details:** Deceased donor, two HLA mismatches (A and B loci). HLA mismatches are the primary drivers of alloimmune responses.
*   **Immunosuppression:** Tacrolimus (calcineurin inhibitor, inhibits T cell activation), mycophenolate mofetil (inhibits purine synthesis, affects lymphocyte proliferation), prednisone (corticosteroid, broad immunosuppressant). Despite immunosuppression, rejection occurs.
*   **Timing:** Three weeks post-transplant. This timeframe is characteristic of acute rejection (cellular or antibody-mediated).
*   **Clinical Presentation:** Fever, flank pain, decreased urine output, elevated creatinine. These are classic signs of acute graft dysfunction, suggestive of rejection.
*   **Biopsy Findings:** Diffuse interstitial edema, lymphocyte infiltrates, tubular cell injury. This indicates inflammation and damage within the graft.
*   **Immunofluorescence:** C4d deposition in peritubular capillaries. C4d is a marker of complement activation, often seen in antibody-mediated rejection (AMR), but can also be present in acute cellular rejection (ACR) due to bystander activation or overlap syndromes. However, the primary infiltrate is lymphocytes.
*   **Flow Cytometry:** Increased CD8+ T cells infiltrating graft parenchyma, targeting tubular epithelial cells. This is a key finding pointing towards CD8+ CTL-mediated injury.
*   **Serum Analysis:** Normal C3/C4 (argues against classical pathway complement consumption typical of severe AMR), elevated IL-2 and IFN-γ (cytokines produced by activated T cells, particularly Th1 and CTLs).

The constellation of findings – acute onset (3 weeks), clinical signs of graft dysfunction, biopsy showing lymphocytic infiltrate with tubular injury, increased CD8+ T cells targeting parenchymal cells, and elevated T cell cytokines – strongly points towards acute cellular rejection mediated primarily by CD8+ cytotoxic T lymphocytes.

##### 2. Immunological, Pathological & Virulence Deep-Dive (免疫机制、微生物致病机理与分子生物学深度剖析)
*   **Acute Cellular Rejection (ACR):** Primarily T cell-mediated. Can be T cell-mediated rejection (TCMR) or antibody-mediated rejection (AMR). TCMR is further subdivided based on the predominant T cell type and location of infiltrate.
*   **CD8+ T Cell Role:** CD8+ T cells (CTLs) recognize foreign MHC class I molecules (allo-MHC class I) presented on the surface of graft cells (e.g., tubular epithelial cells in the kidney). This recognition, mediated by the T cell receptor (TCR), triggers CTL activation.
*   **CTL Activation & Mechanism:** Activated CTLs release cytotoxic granules containing perforin and granzymes. Perforin forms pores in the target cell membrane, allowing granzymes to enter and activate caspases, leading to apoptosis (programmed cell death) of the target graft cell. CTLs also express Fas ligand (FasL), which binds to Fas receptor on target cells, inducing apoptosis. IFN-γ production by CTLs also contributes to MHC upregulation on graft cells, making them more susceptible to CTL killing, and activates macrophages.
*   **CD4+ T Cell Role:** CD4+ T helper cells recognize foreign MHC class II molecules presented by APCs (like dendritic cells) within the graft. They release cytokines (e.g., IL-2, IFN-γ, TNF-α) that promote inflammation, activate macrophages, and help activate CD8+ T cells and B cells. While CD4+ cells are involved in ACR, the vignette specifically highlights CD8+ infiltration targeting parenchymal cells.
*   **Antibody-Mediated Rejection (AMR):** Involves donor-specific antibodies (DSAs) binding to donor HLA antigens (often MHC class II, but can be class I or non-HLA) on endothelial cells. This triggers complement activation (classical pathway), leading to endothelial damage, inflammation, and microvascular injury (capillaritis, glomerululitis). C4d deposition is a hallmark. While C4d is present here, the dominant infiltrate is lymphocytes targeting parenchymal cells, not primarily endothelial cells, and the primary mechanism highlighted is CTL-mediated.
*   **Chronic Rejection:** Occurs later (months to years), characterized by fibrosis, vascular thickening (intimal arteritis), and progressive loss of graft function. It involves both cellular and humoral immunity, with CD4+ T cells, B cells, and fibroblasts playing key roles.
*   **Hyperacute Rejection:** Occurs within minutes to hours, mediated by pre-formed antibodies (e.g., anti-ABO, anti-HLA) causing immediate endothelial damage and thrombosis. Not consistent with the 3-week timeframe.

The vignette strongly supports CD8+ CTL-mediated acute cellular rejection targeting parenchymal cells (tubular epithelial cells).

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A): This describes Antibody-Mediated Rejection (AMR). While C4d deposition can be seen in AMR, the primary histological finding here is lymphocytic infiltrate targeting *parenchymal* cells (tubular cells), not primarily endothelial cells. The flow cytometry specifically identifies increased CD8+ T cells in the parenchyma. AMR typically involves DSAs binding MHC class II on endothelial cells, leading to complement activation and endothelial injury (endotheliitis). This option is incorrect because the primary mechanism described in the vignette is cellular, targeting parenchymal cells via CD8+ T cells.
- (B): This describes CD4+ T cell-mediated ACR. CD4+ T cells are involved in ACR, recognizing MHC class II on APCs and releasing cytokines. However, the vignette specifically highlights the infiltration and targeting of *parenchymal* cells (tubular epithelial cells) by *CD8+* T cells. While CD4+ cells contribute, the direct cell lysis mechanism described is characteristic of CD8+ CTLs. This option is incorrect because it focuses on CD4+ cells and APCs, whereas the key finding is CD8+ cells targeting parenchymal cells.
- (D): This describes Chronic Rejection. Chronic rejection occurs much later (months to years) and is characterized by fibrosis and vascular changes, not the acute presentation and cellular infiltrate described here. This option is incorrect due to the timing and histological features.
- (E): This describes Hyperacute Rejection. Hyperacute rejection occurs within minutes to hours due to pre-formed antibodies and causes immediate thrombosis. This is inconsistent with the 3-week timeframe and the described pathology. This option is incorrect due to the timing and mechanism.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点与易混淆病原体/免疫缺陷横向对比):
| Feature             | Acute Cellular Rejection (CD8+ mediated) | Antibody-Mediated Rejection (AMR) | Chronic Rejection | Hyperacute Rejection |
| :------------------ | :--------------------------------------- | :-------------------------------- | :---------------- | :------------------- |
| **Timing**          | Days to weeks post-transplant             | Days to weeks post-transplant     | Months to years  | Minutes to hours     |
| **Primary Mediator** | CD8+ Cytotoxic T Lymphocytes (CTLs)      | Donor-Specific Antibodies (DSAs)  | CD4+ T cells, B cells, Fibroblasts | Pre-formed Antibodies |
| **Target Cells**     | Graft Parenchymal Cells (e.g., tubules)  | Endothelial Cells                 | Vessels, Parenchyma | Endothelial Cells    |
| **Key Histology**   | Lymphocytic infiltrate in parenchyma, tubular injury | Endotheliitis, Capillaritis, Glomerulitis, C4d deposition | Fibrosis, Vascular thickening (intimal arteritis) | Thrombosis, Necrosis  |
| **Key Immune Cells** | CD8+ T cells                             | B cells, Macrophages              | CD4+ T cells      | B cells              |
| **Mechanism**       | Direct cell lysis (perforin/granzymes, FasL) | Complement activation, ADCC      | Fibrosis, Inflammation | Complement activation, Thrombosis |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
Acute cellular rejection is primarily mediated by T cells. CD8+ cytotoxic T lymphocytes (CTLs) directly kill graft parenchymal cells expressing allo-MHC class I molecules. Antibody-mediated rejection involves DSAs targeting endothelial cells, leading to complement activation and endothelial damage. Differentiating between ACR and AMR based on clinical presentation, biopsy findings (location of infiltrate, C4d deposition), and immunological markers (CD8+ T cells vs. DSAs) is crucial for appropriate management. Remember the typical timing for each type of rejection.

<a id='question-133'></a>

### Question 133: Transplantation: Part 3 Focus 33 (according)
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Understanding the immunological mechanisms underlying acute cellular rejection in solid organ transplantation, specifically the role of T lymphocytes (CD8+ cytotoxic T cells) and their recognition of alloantigens presented by MHC class I molecules on graft cells. (理解实体器官移植中急性细胞性排斥的免疫机制，特别是T淋巴细胞（CD8+细胞毒性T细胞）的作用及其对移植细胞表面MHC I类分子呈递的异体抗原的识别。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 52-year-old male with end-stage renal disease due to diabetic nephropathy receives a kidney transplant from a deceased donor with one HLA mismatch at the A locus. He is started on standard immunosuppressive therapy including tacrolimus, mycophenolate mofetil, and prednisone. Three weeks post-transplant, he develops fever, flank pain, decreased urine output, and elevated serum creatinine. A renal biopsy reveals interstitial edema, inflammatory cell infiltrates primarily composed of lymphocytes, and evidence of tubular injury. Immunofluorescence staining shows C4d deposition in peritubular capillaries. Flow cytometry analysis of the biopsy sample identifies a significant increase in CD8+ T cells infiltrating the graft tubules and interstitium, many of which are expressing Fas ligand (FasL). The patient's serum creatinine continues to rise despite dose adjustments of immunosuppressants.

Which of the following mechanisms is MOST likely responsible for the observed graft injury in this patient?

(A) Antibody-mediated rejection involving donor-specific antibodies (DSA) against HLA class II antigens, leading to complement activation and endothelial damage.
(B) Acute cellular rejection mediated by CD4+ T helper cells recognizing donor HLA class II antigens presented by recipient antigen-presenting cells (APCs).
(C) Acute cellular rejection mediated by CD8+ cytotoxic T lymphocytes (CTLs) recognizing donor HLA class I antigens presented directly on graft cells.
(D) Chronic rejection characterized by fibrosis and vascular changes, typically occurring months to years after transplantation.
(E) Hyperacute rejection occurring within minutes to hours of transplantation due to pre-formed anti-donor antibodies.

#### Correct Answer: C

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical/Microbiological Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Profile:** 52-year-old male, end-stage renal disease (ESRD) from diabetic nephropathy, kidney transplant recipient. This sets the stage for potential transplant complications.
- **Transplant Details:** Deceased donor kidney, one HLA mismatch (A locus). HLA mismatch increases the risk of rejection.
- **Immunosuppression:** Standard triple therapy (tacrolimus, mycophenolate mofetil, prednisone). Despite this, rejection occurs.
- **Timeline:** Three weeks post-transplant. This timeframe is characteristic of acute rejection (cellular or antibody-mediated).
- **Clinical Presentation:** Fever, flank pain, decreased urine output, elevated creatinine. These are classic signs of acute graft dysfunction, suggestive of rejection.
- **Biopsy Findings:** Interstitial edema, lymphocyte infiltrates, tubular injury. This confirms acute rejection.
- **Immunofluorescence:** C4d deposition in peritubular capillaries. C4d is a marker of complement activation, often seen in antibody-mediated rejection (AMR), but can also be present in acute cellular rejection (ACR) due to endothelial injury.
- **Flow Cytometry:** Increased CD8+ T cells infiltrating tubules/interstitium, expressing FasL. This is a key finding pointing towards CD8+ CTL-mediated cytotoxicity.
- **Clinical Course:** Rising creatinine despite immunosuppression adjustment. Indicates ongoing rejection process.

The combination of acute onset (3 weeks), clinical signs of graft dysfunction, biopsy showing lymphocytic infiltrates and tubular injury, and crucially, the presence of CD8+ T cells expressing FasL infiltrating the graft points strongly towards acute cellular rejection mediated by CD8+ cytotoxic T lymphocytes.

##### 2. Immunological, Pathological & Virulence Deep-Dive (免疫机制、微生物致病机理与分子生物学深度剖析)
- **Acute Cellular Rejection (ACR):** Primarily mediated by T lymphocytes recognizing donor alloantigens.
    - **CD4+ T cells:** Recognize donor MHC class II molecules presented by recipient APCs (like dendritic cells). They activate macrophages and help CD8+ T cells.
    - **CD8+ T cells:** Recognize donor MHC class I molecules presented directly on graft cells (tubular epithelial cells, endothelial cells). Upon recognition, CD8+ CTLs kill the target graft cells.
    - **Mechanism of CD8+ CTL Killing:**
        1.  **Recognition:** T cell receptor (TCR) on CD8+ T cell binds to the peptide-MHC class I complex on the target cell.
        2.  **Activation:** Co-stimulatory signals (e.g., CD28-B7) are required for full activation.
        3.  **Cytotoxicity:** Activated CD8+ CTLs release cytotoxic granules containing perforin and granzymes. Perforin forms pores in the target cell membrane, allowing granzymes to enter and activate caspases, leading to apoptosis.
        4.  **FasL/Fas Pathway:** CD8+ CTLs also express Fas ligand (FasL), which binds to Fas (CD95) receptors on target cells, triggering the extrinsic apoptotic pathway. This is a major mechanism of CTL-mediated killing.
- **Antibody-Mediated Rejection (AMR):** Mediated by antibodies (DSAs) against donor HLA antigens.
    - **Mechanisms:** Complement activation (classical pathway), antibody-dependent cell-mediated cytotoxicity (ADCC) by NK cells, opsonization and phagocytosis, endothelial cell activation.
    - **Markers:** C4d deposition in peritubular capillaries is a hallmark, but not exclusive to AMR.
- **Hyperacute Rejection:** Occurs within minutes/hours due to pre-formed antibodies against donor ABO or HLA antigens. Leads to rapid thrombosis and graft necrosis.
- **Chronic Rejection:** Occurs months/years post-transplant. Characterized by fibrosis, vascular changes (graft arteriosclerosis), and progressive loss of graft function.

In this case, the biopsy shows lymphocytic infiltrates (characteristic of ACR) and the flow cytometry specifically identifies increased CD8+ T cells expressing FasL within the graft parenchyma (tubules/interstitium), directly implicating CD8+ CTL-mediated cytotoxicity as the primary mechanism of injury.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A): Antibody-mediated rejection involves DSAs, typically against HLA class II (though class I DSAs exist). While C4d deposition is present (suggestive of complement activation often seen in AMR), the dominant finding is the infiltration of CD8+ T cells expressing FasL, which is the hallmark of CTL-mediated killing in ACR. AMR typically involves endothelial damage and inflammation, often with more prominent C4d deposition and potentially microvascular inflammation (glomerulitis, peritubular capillaritis). The primary effector cells in this vignette are CD8+ T cells, not antibodies.
- (B): CD4+ T cells are involved in ACR, primarily by activating macrophages and helping CD8+ T cells. However, the direct killing of graft cells (tubular epithelial cells) is predominantly mediated by CD8+ CTLs recognizing MHC class I. The specific finding of CD8+ T cells expressing FasL points directly to CD8+ CTLs as the main effector cells causing the observed tubular injury. While CD4+ cells contribute, they are not the *most likely* direct cause of the cell death described.
- (D): Chronic rejection occurs much later (months to years) and involves fibrosis and vascular changes, not the acute lymphocytic infiltrate and tubular injury seen here. The timeline (3 weeks) is inconsistent with chronic rejection.
- (E): Hyperacute rejection occurs within minutes to hours due to pre-formed antibodies and leads to immediate graft thrombosis. This patient's rejection occurred at 3 weeks, ruling out hyperacute rejection.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点与易混淆病原体/免疫缺陷横向对比):
| Feature                 | Acute Cellular Rejection (CD8+ CTL) | Antibody-Mediated Rejection (AMR) | Hyperacute Rejection | Chronic Rejection |
| :---------------------- | :--------------------------------- | :-------------------------------- | :------------------- | :---------------- |
| **Timeline**            | Days to weeks                     | Days to weeks                     | Minutes to hours    | Months to years   |
| **Primary Effector**    | CD8+ T cells (CTLs)                | Donor-Specific Antibodies (DSAs)  | Pre-formed Abs      | Fibrosis, Vascular |
| **Key Mechanism**       | CTL-mediated cytotoxicity (FasL/Perforin/Granzymes) | Complement activation, ADCC      | Thrombosis          | Fibrosis          |
| **Biopsy Findings**     | Lymphocytic infiltrate, tubular injury | Microvascular inflammation, C4d+ | Thrombosis          | Fibrosis, Vascular |
| **Key Cell Markers**    | CD8+, FasL+                        | DSAs, C4d                          | Pre-formed Abs      | N/A               |
| **Typical Presentation** | Acute graft dysfunction            | Acute graft dysfunction            | Immediate graft loss | Gradual decline   |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
Acute cellular rejection is primarily mediated by T lymphocytes. CD8+ cytotoxic T lymphocytes (CTLs) directly kill graft cells expressing alloantigens (MHC class I), often via FasL/Fas interaction or perforin/granzyme release. Antibody-mediated rejection involves DSAs and complement activation (C4d deposition). Differentiating between ACR and AMR based on biopsy findings (lymphocyte type, location, C4d) and clinical context is crucial for management. Remember the timeline: Hyperacute (minutes), Acute (days-weeks), Chronic (months-years).

<a id='question-134'></a>

### Question 134: Transplantation: Part 3 Focus 34 (activation)
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Understanding the role of T cell activation, specifically the requirement for co-stimulation (CD28-B7 interaction) in preventing anergy and promoting effective immune responses, including graft rejection. (理解T细胞激活中，共刺激（CD28-B7相互作用）在防止无能状态和促进有效免疫反应（包括移植物排斥）中的作用。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 52-year-old male with end-stage renal disease due to diabetic nephropathy receives a kidney transplant from a deceased donor. His pre-transplant panel reactive antibody (PRA) score was 10%, indicating low sensitization. Post-transplant, he is maintained on a standard immunosuppressive regimen including tacrolimus, mycophenolate mofetil, and prednisone. Six months after transplantation, he develops acute onset fever, flank pain, and decreased urine output. Serum creatinine rises from a baseline of 1.2 mg/dL to 3.5 mg/dL. A renal biopsy reveals acute tubular necrosis and interstitial inflammation with infiltration of T lymphocytes. Flow cytometry analysis of the biopsy sample shows a significant increase in CD3+ T cells expressing CD25 (IL-2 receptor alpha chain) and HLA-DR. However, the expression of CD28 on these activated T cells is markedly reduced compared to pre-transplant levels. The patient's immunosuppression is intensified, and his renal function gradually improves. Which of the following mechanisms is most likely responsible for the observed reduction in CD28 expression on activated T cells in this patient's kidney graft?

(A) Downregulation of CD28 expression by chronic exposure to tacrolimus, leading to impaired T cell activation and graft rejection.
(B) Upregulation of CTLA-4 (CD152) on activated T cells, which competes with CD28 for binding to B7 ligands, leading to reduced CD28 signaling and T cell anergy.
(C) Increased expression of PD-1 (CD279) on activated T cells, which inhibits T cell activation by binding to PD-L1 on renal tubular epithelial cells, leading to graft dysfunction.
(D) Enhanced expression of B7-1 (CD80) and B7-2 (CD86) ligands on antigen-presenting cells (APCs) within the graft, leading to increased CD28 engagement and T cell activation.
(E) Induction of regulatory T cells (Tregs) expressing high levels of CD25 and CTLA-4, which suppress the activation of effector T cells through IL-10 and TGF-β production.

#### Correct Answer: B

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical/Microbiological Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Profile:** 52-year-old male, end-stage renal disease, kidney transplant recipient. This sets the stage for potential graft rejection.
- **Immunosuppression:** Standard regimen (tacrolimus, mycophenolate mofetil, prednisone). This indicates an attempt to prevent rejection, but rejection can still occur.
- **Clinical Presentation:** Acute onset fever, flank pain, decreased urine output, rising creatinine (acute graft dysfunction). This strongly suggests acute rejection.
- **Biopsy Findings:** Acute tubular necrosis, interstitial inflammation with T lymphocyte infiltration. Confirms acute cellular rejection.
- **Flow Cytometry:** Increased CD3+ T cells, increased CD25 (IL-2Rα) and HLA-DR (signs of T cell activation). This confirms T cell activation within the graft.
- **Key Finding:** Markedly reduced CD28 expression on these activated T cells compared to pre-transplant levels. This is the central clue pointing towards a specific regulatory mechanism.
- **Outcome:** Intensified immunosuppression leads to improvement. This supports the diagnosis of rejection and the effectiveness of further immunosuppression.
- **Question Stem:** Asks for the mechanism responsible for the reduced CD28 expression.

##### 2. Immunological, Pathological & Virulence Deep-Dive (免疫机制、微生物致病机理与分子生物学深度剖析)
- **T Cell Activation:** Naive T cells require two signals for full activation: Signal 1 (TCR engagement with MHC-peptide complex) and Signal 2 (co-stimulation, typically CD28 on T cell binding to B7-1/B7-2 on APCs).
- **CD28:** A crucial co-stimulatory receptor on T cells. Its engagement by B7 ligands is essential for T cell proliferation, cytokine production (like IL-2), and effector function. Without co-stimulation (Signal 2), T cells may become anergic (unresponsive) or undergo apoptosis.
- **CTLA-4 (CD152):** An inhibitory receptor expressed on activated T cells (and Tregs). It has a higher affinity for B7 ligands (CD80/CD86) than CD28. Upon T cell activation, CTLA-4 is upregulated and translocates to the immunological synapse, outcompeting CD28 for B7 binding. This reduces CD28 signaling, dampens T cell activation, and promotes T cell tolerance/anergy. This is a key negative regulatory mechanism.
- **PD-1 (CD279):** Another inhibitory receptor expressed on activated T cells, Tregs, and exhausted T cells. It binds to PD-L1 (CD274) and PD-L2 on APCs and other cells. PD-1 signaling inhibits T cell activation, proliferation, and cytokine production, contributing to T cell exhaustion in chronic settings.
- **B7 Ligands (CD80/CD86):** Co-stimulatory molecules expressed on APCs. Their expression is upregulated upon APC activation. They bind to CD28 (promoting activation) and CTLA-4 (inhibiting activation).
- **Tacrolimus:** A calcineurin inhibitor that primarily blocks IL-2 production by inhibiting T cell activation. While it can affect T cell phenotype, it doesn't directly cause the specific reduction in CD28 expression described, nor is it the primary mechanism for the observed phenomenon.
- **Regulatory T cells (Tregs):** A subset of T cells (typically CD4+CD25+) that suppress immune responses. They express high levels of CTLA-4 and produce inhibitory cytokines (IL-10, TGF-β). While Tregs are involved in graft tolerance, the vignette describes reduced CD28 on *activated* T cells infiltrating the graft, not necessarily the primary mechanism of Treg suppression itself. The question focuses on the mechanism affecting the effector T cells causing the rejection.
- **Connecting Clues:** The patient has acute cellular rejection (T cell infiltrate, biopsy findings). The T cells are activated (CD25+, HLA-DR+). The key finding is *reduced* CD28 expression on these activated cells. This points towards a negative regulatory mechanism involving competition for B7 ligands. CTLA-4 upregulation fits this perfectly: activated T cells upregulate CTLA-4, which outcompetes CD28 for B7, leading to reduced CD28 signaling and potentially anergy or dampened response, although in this context, it's occurring *during* an active rejection episode, likely as a counter-regulatory mechanism or a sign of T cell exhaustion/dysfunction.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A): **Incorrect.** Tacrolimus primarily inhibits IL-2 production by blocking calcineurin. While chronic exposure can affect T cell phenotype, it doesn't specifically cause the observed *reduction* in CD28 expression as the primary mechanism of rejection or T cell dysfunction in this acute scenario. The vignette describes an *acute* rejection episode with activated T cells.
- (C): **Incorrect.** PD-1 is an inhibitory receptor, but its primary role is often associated with T cell exhaustion in chronic infections or tumors. While it can play a role in transplantation, the specific finding of *reduced CD28* expression is more directly linked to CTLA-4 competition for B7 ligands. PD-1 inhibits downstream signaling *after* TCR and CD28 engagement, whereas CTLA-4 directly competes for the co-stimulatory signal (B7).
- (D): **Incorrect.** Increased B7 expression on APCs would *enhance* CD28 engagement, leading to *stronger* T cell activation, not reduced CD28 expression. This would promote, not inhibit, the rejection response.
- (E): **Incorrect.** While Tregs (CD25+, CTLA-4+) are important in transplantation and express high CTLA-4, the vignette describes reduced CD28 expression on the *effector T cells* infiltrating the graft (CD3+ T cells), not necessarily the primary mechanism of Treg suppression itself. Furthermore, Tregs are generally associated with *suppressing* rejection, whereas the clinical picture is one of active rejection. The question asks about the mechanism affecting the *activated effector T cells* causing the rejection.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点与易混淆病原体/免疫缺陷横向对比):
| Feature          | Correct Answer (B - CTLA-4)                                  | Distractor (A - Tacrolimus)                                  | Distractor (C - PD-1)                                       | Distractor (D - Increased B7)                             | Distractor (E - Tregs)                                     |
|-------------------|--------------------------------------------------------------|--------------------------------------------------------------|--------------------------------------------------------------|--------------------------------------------------------------|--------------------------------------------------------------|
| **Mechanism**     | Inhibitory receptor; competes with CD28 for B7.             | Immunosuppressant; inhibits calcineurin/IL-2 production.      | Inhibitory receptor; inhibits T cell function post-activation. | Enhances T cell activation via CD28 co-stimulation.        | Suppresses immune response via CTLA-4/IL-10/TGF-β.           |
| **Effect on CD28**| Reduces CD28 signaling by outcompeting for B7.             | Primarily affects IL-2 production, not direct CD28 effect. | Inhibits downstream signaling, not direct B7 competition.   | Increases CD28 signaling.                                  | Expresses high CTLA-4, but mechanism is Treg suppression. |
| **Relevance**     | Negative regulation of T cell activation, limits rejection. | Drug mechanism, not the specific cellular process described. | T cell exhaustion, less direct link to CD28 reduction here. | Promotes, not inhibits, T cell activation/rejection.        | Role in tolerance, not the mechanism on effector T cells.   |
| **Key Marker**    | CTLA-4 (CD152)                                                | N/A                                                          | PD-1 (CD279)                                                  | B7-1 (CD80), B7-2 (CD86)                                    | CTLA-4 (CD152), CD25                                         |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
Understanding the critical role of co-stimulation (CD28-B7) in T cell activation and the importance of inhibitory receptors like CTLA-4 in regulating T cell responses and preventing excessive immune activation, including graft rejection. Differentiate CTLA-4 (competes with CD28 for B7) from PD-1 (inhibits downstream signaling) and the effects of immunosuppressants like tacrolimus. Recognize that reduced CD28 expression on activated T cells is a hallmark of CTLA-4 upregulation as a negative feedback mechanism.

---

<a id='question-135'></a>

### Question 135: Transplantation: Part 3 Focus 35 (effector)
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Understanding the role of effector T cells (specifically CD8+ cytotoxic T lymphocytes, CTLs) in acute cellular rejection of transplanted organs, including their mechanism of action (perforin/granzyme pathway) and the target cells (allogeneic MHC class I-expressing cells on graft cells). (理解效应器T细胞（特别是CD8+细胞毒性T淋巴细胞，CTL）在移植器官急性细胞性排斥中的作用，包括其作用机制（穿孔素/颗粒酶途径）和靶细胞（移植物细胞上的异基因MHC I类表达细胞）。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 52-year-old male with end-stage renal disease due to diabetic nephropathy receives a kidney transplant from a deceased donor with one HLA mismatch at the A locus. Post-transplant, he is maintained on standard immunosuppression with tacrolimus, mycophenolate mofetil, and prednisone. Three weeks after transplantation, he develops acute onset fever, flank pain, elevated serum creatinine (from baseline 1.2 mg/dL to 4.5 mg/dL), and tender induration over the graft site. A renal biopsy is performed. Histological examination reveals diffuse interstitial inflammation with infiltration of mononuclear cells, particularly lymphocytes, and evidence of tubular epithelial cell injury and necrosis. Immunofluorescence staining shows deposition of C4d in peritubular capillaries, consistent with antibody-mediated rejection (AMR). However, flow cytometry analysis of the infiltrating lymphocytes reveals a predominance of CD3+CD8+ T cells expressing granzyme B. Further investigation reveals that the donor kidney cells express the mismatched HLA-A allele on their surface. The patient's immunosuppression is intensified with anti-thymocyte globulin (ATG).

Which of the following cellular mechanisms is MOST likely responsible for the observed graft injury in this patient?

(A) Antibody-dependent cell-mediated cytotoxicity (ADCC) mediated by NK cells recognizing graft cells coated with donor-specific antibodies (DSAs).
(B) Complement-dependent cytotoxicity (CDC) mediated by MAC formation on graft cells due to binding of DSAs and complement activation.
(C) Perforin and granzyme release by CD8+ cytotoxic T lymphocytes (CTLs) targeting allogeneic MHC class I molecules on graft cells.
(D) Fas ligand (FasL) and Fas interaction between activated CD4+ helper T cells and graft cells, leading to apoptosis.
(E) Neutrophil-mediated inflammation and tissue damage triggered by activation of the alternative complement pathway on the graft endothelium.

#### Correct Answer: C

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical/Microbiological Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Profile:** 52-year-old male, end-stage renal disease, kidney transplant recipient. This sets the context for transplantation immunology.
- **Immunosuppression:** Tacrolimus (calcineurin inhibitor), mycophenolate mofetil (purine synthesis inhibitor), prednisone (corticosteroid). Standard immunosuppression, but rejection can still occur.
- **Timeline:** 3 weeks post-transplant. This timeframe is typical for acute rejection, both cellular and antibody-mediated.
- **Symptoms:** Fever, flank pain, elevated creatinine, graft tenderness. Classic signs of acute rejection.
- **Biopsy Findings:** Interstitial inflammation, lymphocyte infiltration, tubular injury/necrosis. Suggests rejection.
- **Immunofluorescence:** C4d deposition in peritubular capillaries. This is a hallmark of antibody-mediated rejection (AMR), indicating complement activation.
- **Flow Cytometry:** Predominance of CD3+CD8+ T cells expressing granzyme B in the infiltrate. This points strongly towards a cellular component, specifically cytotoxic T lymphocytes (CTLs).
- **HLA Mismatch:** One HLA-A mismatch. This provides a target for T cell recognition (MHC class I).
- **Treatment:** Intensified immunosuppression with ATG. ATG targets T cells, further supporting a T cell-mediated process.
- **Question Stem:** Asks for the MOST likely cellular mechanism responsible for graft injury.

The vignette presents a complex picture with features of both AMR (C4d deposition) and cellular rejection (CD8+ T cell infiltrate, granzyme B expression, HLA mismatch). However, the question specifically asks for the mechanism related to the *cellular* component, highlighted by the CD8+ T cell infiltrate and granzyme B expression. CD8+ CTLs recognize foreign MHC class I molecules (like the mismatched HLA-A) on graft cells and kill them. Their primary mechanism involves the release of perforin (which forms pores in the target cell membrane) and granzymes (serine proteases that enter the target cell via the pores and induce apoptosis).

##### 2. Immunological, Pathological & Virulence Deep-Dive (免疫机制、微生物致病机理与分子生物学深度剖析)
- **Acute Cellular Rejection:** Primarily mediated by T cells (CD4+ and CD8+). CD8+ CTLs directly kill graft cells expressing foreign MHC class I molecules. CD4+ T cells can help activate CTLs and macrophages, contributing to inflammation and tissue damage.
- **CTL Mechanism:**
    - **Recognition:** T cell receptor (TCR) on CD8+ T cell recognizes peptide antigen presented by MHC class I molecule on the target cell surface.
    - **Activation:** Co-stimulatory signals (e.g., CD28-B7) are required for full activation.
    - **Effector Functions:**
        - **Perforin/Granzyme Pathway:** CTLs release perforin (forms pores) and granzymes (enter pores, activate caspases leading to apoptosis). This is the primary cytotoxic mechanism.
        - **Fas/FasL Pathway:** CTLs express Fas ligand (FasL), which binds to Fas (CD95) receptor on target cells, triggering apoptosis. This pathway is also important but often considered secondary to perforin/granzyme.
- **Antibody-Mediated Rejection (AMR):** Mediated by antibodies (DSAs) against donor HLA antigens. Mechanisms include:
    - **Complement-Dependent Cytotoxicity (CDC):** DSAs activate complement, leading to MAC formation and cell lysis.
    - **ADCC:** NK cells or other effector cells bind to DSAs coating graft cells via Fc receptors, leading to target cell lysis.
    - **Inflammation:** Antibody binding activates endothelial cells and recruits inflammatory cells.
- **C4d Deposition:** A marker of complement activation, strongly associated with AMR.
- **Granzyme B:** A serine protease released by CTLs and NK cells, crucial for inducing apoptosis in target cells. Its presence in the infiltrate confirms cytotoxic activity.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A): Antibody-dependent cell-mediated cytotoxicity (ADCC) involves NK cells (or other Fc receptor-bearing cells) recognizing antibody-coated target cells. While AMR is present (C4d deposition), the predominant infiltrate is CD8+ T cells expressing granzyme B, pointing to a CTL-mediated mechanism, not ADCC by NK cells. NK cells are CD3-.
- (B): Complement-dependent cytotoxicity (CDC) is a mechanism of AMR where antibodies activate complement, leading to MAC formation and cell lysis. While C4d deposition indicates complement activation, the primary effector cells identified in the infiltrate are CD8+ CTLs, whose main mechanism is perforin/granzyme, not CDC. CDC is mediated by complement components, not directly by the infiltrating lymphocytes.
- (D): Fas ligand (FasL) and Fas interaction is a mechanism used by CTLs to induce apoptosis. However, the perforin/granzyme pathway is generally considered the *primary* and most potent cytotoxic mechanism of CTLs. While FasL contributes, option (C) describes the main pathway associated with granzyme B expression by CD8+ CTLs. Furthermore, FasL is expressed by both CD4+ and CD8+ T cells, whereas the infiltrate is predominantly CD8+.
- (E): Neutrophil-mediated inflammation is characteristic of acute inflammation and can occur in rejection, but it's not the specific mechanism mediated by the identified CD8+ T cells. The alternative complement pathway can activate neutrophils, but the primary cytotoxic mechanism of the infiltrating CTLs is perforin/granzyme. Neutrophils are not the main effector cells in this specific scenario described by the CD8+ infiltrate and granzyme B.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点与易混淆病原体/免疫缺陷横向对比):
| Feature                 | Acute Cellular Rejection (CTL-mediated) | Antibody-Mediated Rejection (AMR) | ADCC                                  | CDC                                  | Fas/FasL Interaction                |
| :---------------------- | :-------------------------------------- | :-------------------------------- | :------------------------------------ | :------------------------------------ | :------------------------------------ |
| **Primary Effector Cell** | CD8+ Cytotoxic T Lymphocytes (CTLs)     | Antibodies (DSAs)                 | NK Cells                             | Complement Components                | CD8+ & CD4+ T cells, other cells      |
| **Target Antigen**      | MHC Class I (allogeneic)                | Donor HLA antigens                | Antibody-coated cells                 | Antibody-coated cells                 | Fas Receptor                         |
| **Key Mechanism**       | Perforin/Granzyme release                | Complement activation, ADCC, Inflammation | Fc receptor binding, release of cytotoxic granules | MAC formation, inflammation          | FasL binding to Fas receptor         |
| **Hallmark Markers**    | CD8+ infiltrate, Granzyme B expression  | C4d deposition, DSAs              | NK cells, DSAs                        | C4d deposition, DSAs                  | FasL expression                      |
| **Biopsy Findings**     | Interstitial inflammation, cell injury   | Capillary inflammation, C4d       | Variable, often less severe injury    | Variable, often less severe injury    | Variable, often less severe injury    |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
Acute cellular rejection is primarily mediated by CD8+ CTLs recognizing foreign MHC class I on graft cells, leading to target cell death via the perforin/granzyme pathway. While AMR can coexist (as evidenced by C4d), the question focuses on the mechanism driven by the specific infiltrating cells (CD8+ CTLs expressing granzyme B). Differentiate CTL-mediated cytotoxicity (perforin/granzyme) from AMR mechanisms (CDC, ADCC) and other T cell-mediated apoptosis pathways (Fas/FasL).
