<a id='question-136'></a>

### Question 136: Transplantation: Part 3 Focus 36 (Mechanism)
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Understanding the mechanism of acute cellular rejection in organ transplantation, specifically the role of T lymphocytes (CD8+ cytotoxic T cells and CD4+ helper T cells) in recognizing alloantigens presented by MHC molecules on graft cells, leading to direct cell killing and cytokine release. (理解器官移植中急性细胞性排斥的机制，特别是T淋巴细胞（CD8+细胞毒性T细胞和CD4+辅助T细胞）在识别移植细胞上MHC分子呈递的异体抗原中的作用，导致直接细胞杀伤和细胞因子释放。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 52-year-old male with end-stage renal disease due to diabetic nephropathy receives a kidney transplant from a deceased donor with one HLA mismatch at the HLA-B locus. Post-transplant, he is maintained on immunosuppressive therapy consisting of tacrolimus, mycophenolate mofetil, and prednisone. Three weeks after transplantation, he develops fever, flank pain, decreased urine output, and elevated serum creatinine. A renal biopsy reveals interstitial edema, inflammatory cell infiltrates primarily composed of lymphocytes, and evidence of tubular epithelial cell damage. Immunofluorescence staining shows deposition of C4d in peritubular capillaries. Flow cytometry analysis of the biopsy sample shows a significant increase in CD8+ T cells infiltrating the graft parenchyma, particularly around damaged tubules. Serum levels of IL-2 and IFN-γ are elevated.

Which of the following cellular mechanisms is MOST directly responsible for the observed tubular epithelial cell damage in this patient?

(A) Antibody-dependent cell-mediated cytotoxicity (ADCC) mediated by NK cells recognizing antibody-coated graft cells.
(B) Complement-dependent cytotoxicity (CDC) initiated by donor-specific antibodies binding to MHC class I molecules on graft endothelial cells.
(C) Direct cytotoxicity mediated by CD8+ T lymphocytes recognizing donor HLA-B antigens presented on MHC class I molecules of graft tubular epithelial cells.
(D) Macrophage-mediated phagocytosis of graft cells opsonized by antibodies and complement components.
(E) Neutrophil-mediated inflammation and tissue damage triggered by activation of the alternative complement pathway on graft surfaces.

#### Correct Answer: C

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical/Microbiological Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Profile:** 52-year-old male, end-stage renal disease (ESRD) from diabetic nephropathy, kidney transplant recipient. This sets the stage for potential graft rejection.
- **Transplant Details:** Deceased donor kidney, one HLA-B mismatch. HLA mismatch is a major risk factor for alloimmune rejection.
- **Immunosuppression:** Tacrolimus (calcineurin inhibitor), mycophenolate mofetil (purine synthesis inhibitor), prednisone (corticosteroid). Despite immunosuppression, rejection can still occur.
- **Clinical Presentation:** 3 weeks post-transplant, fever, flank pain, decreased urine output, elevated creatinine. These are classic signs and symptoms of acute graft rejection. The timing (3 weeks) is typical for acute cellular rejection.
- **Biopsy Findings:** Interstitial edema, lymphocyte infiltrates (primarily lymphocytes), tubular epithelial cell damage. This points towards cellular rejection affecting the tubules.
- **Immunofluorescence:** C4d deposition in peritubular capillaries. C4d is a marker of complement activation, often seen in antibody-mediated rejection (AMR), but can also be present in acute cellular rejection (ACR) due to microvascular inflammation (glomerulitis or peritubular capillaritis). Its presence doesn't exclude ACR.
- **Flow Cytometry:** Increased CD8+ T cells infiltrating graft parenchyma, especially around damaged tubules. This is a hallmark of acute cellular rejection, specifically cytotoxic T cell-mediated damage.
- **Serum Cytokines:** Elevated IL-2 and IFN-γ. These are key cytokines produced by activated T cells (especially CD8+ and Th1 CD4+ cells), further supporting T cell-mediated rejection.
- **Question Stem:** Asks for the MOST direct mechanism of tubular epithelial cell damage.

##### 2. Immunological, Pathological & Virulence Deep-Dive (免疫机制、微生物致病机理与分子生物学深度剖析)
- **Acute Cellular Rejection (ACR):** Primarily mediated by T lymphocytes recognizing donor MHC molecules (alloantigens) as foreign.
    - **Mechanism:** Recipient T cells (both CD4+ helper T cells and CD8+ cytotoxic T cells) recognize donor MHC molecules presenting peptides on the surface of graft cells.
    - **CD8+ T cells:** Recognize donor MHC class I molecules (present on almost all nucleated cells, including tubular epithelial cells) presenting donor peptides. Upon recognition, CD8+ T cells directly kill the target cells through mechanisms like perforin/granzyme release and Fas/FasL interaction. This is the primary mechanism for direct damage to graft parenchymal cells like tubules.
    - **CD4+ T cells:** Recognize donor MHC class II molecules (present primarily on antigen-presenting cells like dendritic cells, macrophages, and B cells, but also induced on other cells during inflammation). They release cytokines (like IL-2, IFN-γ, TNF-α) that amplify the immune response, activate macrophages, and contribute to inflammation and tissue damage. They can also help CD8+ T cells.
    - **Clinical Manifestations:** Fever, graft dysfunction (e.g., decreased urine output, elevated creatinine in kidney transplant), tenderness over the graft.
    - **Histopathology:** Lymphocytic infiltrates, interstitial edema, parenchymal cell damage (e.g., tubular injury).
    - **Markers:** Increased CD8+ T cells in the graft, elevated IL-2 and IFN-γ. C4d deposition can be present but is more characteristic of AMR.
- **Antibody-Mediated Rejection (AMR):** Mediated by antibodies (donor-specific antibodies, DSAs) against donor HLA antigens.
    - **Mechanisms:**
        - **Complement-Dependent Cytotoxicity (CDC):** Antibodies bind to MHC molecules on endothelial cells, activating the classical complement pathway, leading to membrane attack complex (MAC) formation and cell lysis.
        - **Antibody-Dependent Cell-Mediated Cytotoxicity (ADCC):** Antibodies coat graft cells (often endothelial cells), which are then recognized and killed by effector cells like NK cells (via Fc receptors).
        - **Complement-Mediated Inflammation:** Complement activation (C3a, C5a) recruits inflammatory cells (neutrophils, macrophages) causing tissue damage.
        - **Endothelial Activation:** Antibodies and complement activation damage endothelial cells, leading to microvascular inflammation (glomerulitis, peritubular capillaritis), thrombosis, and ischemia.
    - **Histopathology:** Microvascular inflammation (glomerulitis, peritubular capillaritis), C4d deposition in peritubular capillaries, endothelial cell injury.
    - **Markers:** Presence of DSAs, C4d deposition.

In this case, the prominent finding is CD8+ T cell infiltration directly associated with tubular epithelial cell damage, along with elevated T cell cytokines (IL-2, IFN-γ). This strongly points to acute cellular rejection mediated primarily by CD8+ cytotoxic T cells.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A) **Antibody-dependent cell-mediated cytotoxicity (ADCC) mediated by NK cells recognizing antibody-coated graft cells.** This is a mechanism of Antibody-Mediated Rejection (AMR), not the primary mechanism in this case. While AMR can occur, the dominant findings (CD8+ T cell infiltrate, T cell cytokines, tubular damage) point towards ACR. ADCC involves NK cells, not the primary cytotoxic mechanism described.
- (B) **Complement-dependent cytotoxicity (CDC) initiated by donor-specific antibodies binding to MHC class I molecules on graft endothelial cells.** This is another mechanism of AMR. CDC primarily targets endothelial cells, leading to microvascular injury. The vignette describes damage to tubular epithelial cells, and the primary infiltrate is lymphocytes (T cells), not the typical endothelial inflammation seen in AMR. While C4d is present, it doesn't definitively point to AMR as the *primary* mechanism of *tubular* damage in the context of strong evidence for ACR.
- (D) **Macrophage-mediated phagocytosis of graft cells opsonized by antibodies and complement components.** This is also a mechanism associated with AMR, where antibodies and complement tag graft cells for destruction by macrophages. Again, the vignette's key features (CD8+ T cells, T cell cytokines, direct tubular damage) are more consistent with ACR.
- (E) **Neutrophil-mediated inflammation and tissue damage triggered by activation of the alternative complement pathway on graft surfaces.** Neutrophils are primarily involved in acute inflammation and bacterial infections. While complement activation can recruit neutrophils, this is not the specific mechanism of acute cellular rejection, which is primarily T cell-mediated. The alternative pathway is less commonly implicated in transplant rejection compared to the classical pathway activated by antibodies in AMR.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点与易混淆病原体/免疫缺陷横向对比):
| Feature                 | Acute Cellular Rejection (ACR)                                  | Antibody-Mediated Rejection (AMR)                                     |
| :---------------------- | :------------------------------------------------------------- | :------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **Primary Mediator**    | T lymphocytes (CD8+ cytotoxic T cells, CD4+ helper T cells)     | Antibodies (Donor-Specific Antibodies - DSAs)                         |
| **Target Cells**        | Parenchymal cells (e.g., tubular epithelial cells), endothelial cells | Primarily endothelial cells (glomeruli, peritubular capillaries)        |
| **Key Infiltrate**      | Lymphocytes (CD8+ > CD4+)                                      | Neutrophils, macrophages, lymphocytes (often less prominent than ACR) |
| **Histopathology**      | Interstitial infiltrates, parenchymal cell damage                | Microvascular inflammation (glomerulitis, peritubular capillaritis), C4d deposition |
| **Key Cytokines**       | IL-2, IFN-γ                                                    | -                                                                    |
| **Key Markers**         | Increased CD8+ T cells in graft                                | DSAs, C4d deposition                                                 |
| **Mechanism of Damage** | Direct cytotoxicity (CD8+), cytokine release (CD4+)             | CDC, ADCC, complement-mediated inflammation, endothelial activation |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
Acute cellular rejection is primarily mediated by T cells, particularly CD8+ cytotoxic T cells directly killing graft parenchymal cells. Antibody-mediated rejection involves antibodies targeting graft endothelium, leading to complement activation and inflammation. Differentiating between ACR and AMR is crucial for management, often requiring biopsy and specific immunological testing (DSAs, C4d). Key features pointing to ACR include prominent T cell infiltrates (especially CD8+) and T cell cytokine elevation, while AMR features include microvascular inflammation and C4d deposition.

<a id='question-137'></a>

### Question 137: Transplantation: Part 3 Focus 37 (predominates.)
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Understanding the immunological mechanisms underlying chronic allograft rejection, specifically the role of T-cell mediated vasculopathy and antibody-mediated rejection (AMR), and differentiating them from other causes of graft dysfunction. (理解慢性移植物排斥的免疫机制，特别是T细胞介导的血管病变和抗体介导的排斥，并将其与其他移植物功能障碍的原因区分开来。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 58-year-old male received a kidney transplant 3 years ago for end-stage renal disease secondary to diabetic nephropathy. He has been on chronic immunosuppression with tacrolimus, mycophenolate mofetil, and prednisone. Over the past 6 months, he has experienced a gradual decline in graft function, with rising serum creatinine from a baseline of 1.2 mg/dL to 2.8 mg/dL. He denies fever, chills, or flank pain. Urinalysis shows mild proteinuria (1+). A renal biopsy is performed. Light microscopy reveals interstitial fibrosis and tubular atrophy, along with prominent C4d deposition in peritubular capillaries and glomeruli. Immunofluorescence microscopy shows linear deposition of IgG along the glomerular basement membrane. Electron microscopy shows subendothelial immune complex deposits and evidence of endothelial cell injury. His donor-specific antibody (DSA) panel reactive antibody (PRA) score has increased significantly over the past year. Which of the following is the MOST likely underlying immunological mechanism contributing to this patient's chronic graft dysfunction?

(A) Acute cellular rejection mediated by CD8+ T cells targeting donor MHC class I molecules on tubular epithelial cells.
(B) Antibody-mediated rejection (AMR) characterized by C4d deposition, DSA, and endothelial injury.
(C) Chronic rejection characterized by T-cell mediated vasculopathy involving intimal thickening and fibrosis of graft arterioles.
(D) Tacrolimus-induced thrombotic microangiopathy (TMA) presenting with schistocytes on peripheral blood smear and elevated LDH.
(E) Cytomegalovirus (CMV) infection causing interstitial nephritis and tubular damage.

#### Correct Answer: B

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical/Microbiological Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Profile:** 58-year-old male, 3 years post-kidney transplant for diabetic nephropathy, on chronic immunosuppression (tacrolimus, mycophenolate, prednisone). This sets the stage for potential complications of transplantation, including rejection or drug toxicity.
- **Clinical Presentation:** Gradual decline in graft function (rising creatinine) over 6 months, without acute symptoms (fever, pain). This suggests a chronic process rather than acute rejection or infection. Mild proteinuria is noted.
- **Biopsy Findings:**
    - **Light Microscopy:** Interstitial fibrosis and tubular atrophy (common in chronic rejection, but non-specific).
    - **C4d Deposition:** Prominent C4d deposition in peritubular capillaries and glomeruli. C4d is a breakdown product of the complement C4 protein and is a marker of complement activation, strongly suggesting antibody-mediated injury.
    - **Immunofluorescence:** Linear IgG deposition along the glomerular basement membrane (GBM). This pattern is characteristic of anti-GBM disease, but in the context of transplantation, it can represent *de novo* anti-GBM antibodies or antibodies targeting other glomerular antigens.
    - **Electron Microscopy:** Subendothelial immune complex deposits and endothelial cell injury. This further supports antibody-mediated damage involving immune complex formation and complement activation leading to endothelial damage.
- **Laboratory Findings:** Increased donor-specific antibody (DSA) PRA score. This indicates the presence of antibodies directed against donor HLA antigens, a hallmark of AMR.
- **Question Stem:** Asks for the MOST likely underlying immunological mechanism.

##### 2. Immunological, Pathological & Virulence Deep-Dive (免疫机制、微生物致病机理与分子生物学深度剖析)
- **Antibody-Mediated Rejection (AMR):** This is a major cause of late graft loss. It involves DSAs binding to donor HLA antigens expressed on graft endothelial cells. This binding activates the complement cascade (leading to C4d deposition) and recruits inflammatory cells, causing endothelial injury, microvascular inflammation (glomerulitis, peritubular capillaritis), thrombosis, and ultimately graft dysfunction. The biopsy findings (C4d deposition, DSA, endothelial injury, immune complex deposits) are classic features of AMR. The linear IgG deposition can occur in AMR, although it's more classically associated with anti-GBM disease. In transplant recipients, it can represent antibodies against donor glomerular antigens.
- **Chronic Rejection:** Characterized by T-cell mediated vasculopathy (intimal thickening, fibrosis of arterioles) and interstitial fibrosis/tubular atrophy. While interstitial fibrosis/tubular atrophy is present, the prominent C4d deposition, DSA, and endothelial injury point more strongly towards AMR. T-cell mediated vasculopathy typically doesn't involve significant C4d deposition or DSAs.
- **Acute Cellular Rejection (ACR):** Primarily mediated by T cells (CD4+ and CD8+) recognizing donor MHC molecules. CD8+ T cells directly kill tubular epithelial cells. ACR typically presents acutely (days to weeks post-transplant or after immunosuppression reduction) with rapid rise in creatinine, fever, and graft tenderness. Biopsy shows T-cell infiltrates in tubules and interstitium, often with tubular injury. C4d deposition and DSAs are not characteristic features.
- **Tacrolimus-induced TMA:** Tacrolimus can cause calcineurin inhibitor-associated TMA, characterized by microangiopathic hemolytic anemia (schistocytes), thrombocytopenia, and renal failure. Biopsy shows fibrin thrombi in arterioles and capillaries. C4d deposition and DSAs are not typical.
- **CMV Infection:** CMV can cause interstitial nephritis and tubular damage, leading to graft dysfunction. Biopsy shows characteristic viral inclusions (owl's eye) in endothelial and epithelial cells, often with interstitial inflammation. C4d deposition and DSAs are not features of CMV infection itself, although CMV can sometimes trigger or exacerbate AMR.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A): Incorrect. This describes acute cellular rejection. The patient's presentation is chronic (6 months), and the biopsy findings (C4d deposition, DSA, endothelial injury, immune complexes) are inconsistent with ACR. ACR is primarily T-cell mediated, not antibody-mediated with complement activation.
- (C): Incorrect. This describes chronic rejection, specifically T-cell mediated vasculopathy. While interstitial fibrosis/tubular atrophy is present, the hallmark features of AMR (C4d deposition, DSA, endothelial injury) are more prominent and specific in this case. T-cell mediated vasculopathy typically involves intimal thickening and fibrosis of arterioles, not significant C4d deposition or DSAs.
- (D): Incorrect. This describes tacrolimus-induced TMA. While tacrolimus is part of the patient's regimen, the biopsy findings (C4d deposition, immune complexes, endothelial injury) are not typical for TMA, which involves fibrin thrombi and microangiopathic hemolytic anemia (not mentioned).
- (E): Incorrect. This describes CMV infection. While CMV is a common complication post-transplant, the biopsy findings (C4d deposition, DSA, immune complexes, linear IgG) are not characteristic of CMV nephritis. CMV typically shows viral inclusions.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点与易混淆病原体/免疫缺陷横向对比):
| Feature                 | Antibody-Mediated Rejection (AMR) | Chronic Rejection (T-cell vasculopathy) | Acute Cellular Rejection (ACR) | Tacrolimus TMA | CMV Infection |
| :---------------------- | :-------------------------------- | :-------------------------------------- | :----------------------------- | :------------- | :------------ |
| **Time Course**         | Subacute/Chronic                  | Chronic                                  | Acute                          | Subacute/Chronic | Variable      |
| **Key Finding**         | DSAs, C4d deposition, Endothelial Injury | Intimal thickening, Arteriolar fibrosis | T-cell infiltrates, Tubular injury | Fibrin thrombi | Viral inclusions |
| **Biopsy: C4d**         | Present (peritubular capillaries, glomeruli) | Absent/Minimal                         | Absent                        | Absent         | Absent        |
| **Biopsy: DSA**         | Present                           | Absent                                  | Absent                        | Absent         | Absent        |
| **Biopsy: Endothelial Injury** | Present                           | Minimal                                 | Minimal                        | Present        | Present       |
| **Biopsy: Immune Complexes** | Present (Subendothelial)          | Absent                                  | Absent                        | Absent         | Absent        |
| **Biopsy: Fibrosis/Atrophy** | Present                           | Present                                 | Minimal/Absent                 | Present        | Present       |
| **Immunology**          | Antibodies, Complement             | T cells                                 | T cells                        | Drug toxicity  | Virus         |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
Antibody-mediated rejection (AMR) is a major cause of late kidney graft failure, characterized by donor-specific antibodies (DSAs), complement activation (C4d deposition), and endothelial injury. Differentiating AMR from chronic rejection (T-cell mediated vasculopathy) and other causes like CMV infection or drug toxicity requires careful interpretation of clinical presentation, DSA levels, and renal biopsy findings. Key features of AMR include C4d deposition, DSAs, and evidence of endothelial damage/immune complex deposition.

<a id='question-138'></a>

### Question 138: Transplantation: Part 3 Focus 38 (Hyperacute)
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Hyperacute rejection is a type II hypersensitivity reaction mediated by pre-formed antibodies (IgG, IgM) against donor antigens (e.g., ABO blood group antigens, HLA antigens) present on the graft endothelium, leading to rapid complement activation, endothelial damage, thrombosis, and graft failure within minutes to hours. (超急性排斥反应是一种 II 型超敏反应，由针对供体抗原（如 ABO 血型抗原、HLA 抗原）的预先形成的抗体（IgG、IgM）介导，这些抗原存在于移植物内皮上，导致快速的补体激活、内皮损伤、血栓形成和移植物功能衰竭，发生在数分钟至数小时内。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 58-year-old male with end-stage renal disease secondary to diabetic nephropathy undergoes a kidney transplant from a deceased donor. The patient's pre-transplant blood type is A positive, and the donor's blood type is B positive. The crossmatch test performed prior to transplantation was positive, indicating the presence of pre-formed anti-B antibodies in the recipient's serum. Immediately following the anastomosis of the renal artery and vein, the transplanted kidney becomes mottled, cyanotic, and non-perfused. Within minutes, the patient develops severe hypertension, dyspnea, and signs of disseminated intravascular coagulation (DIC), including petechiae and ecchymoses. A biopsy of the transplanted kidney reveals diffuse thrombosis of glomerular capillaries and peritubular capillaries, along with fibrin deposition in the vessel walls and extensive interstitial hemorrhage. Immunofluorescence staining shows strong deposition of IgM and C3 along the glomerular capillary endothelium.

Which of the following immunological mechanisms is most directly responsible for the observed graft failure?

(A) T-cell mediated cytotoxicity against donor MHC class I molecules on graft endothelial cells.
(B) Antibody-dependent cell-mediated cytotoxicity (ADCC) involving NK cells targeting donor MHC class I molecules.
(C) Complement activation and endothelial damage caused by pre-formed recipient antibodies binding to donor ABO blood group antigens on the graft endothelium.
(D) Formation of immune complexes between donor-specific antibodies and soluble donor antigens, leading to complement activation and neutrophil recruitment.
(E) Direct activation of endothelial cells by donor leukocytes expressing ligands for activating receptors on recipient endothelial cells.

#### Correct Answer: C

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical/Microbiological Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Profile:** 58-year-old male with ESRD due to diabetic nephropathy, undergoing kidney transplant. This sets the stage for transplant rejection.
- **Blood Type Mismatch:** Recipient is A positive, donor is B positive. This is a major ABO incompatibility.
- **Positive Crossmatch:** Indicates pre-formed anti-B antibodies in the recipient's serum. This is a critical clue pointing towards pre-existing immunity against donor antigens.
- **Immediate Graft Changes:** Mottled, cyanotic, non-perfused kidney immediately after anastomosis. This rapid onset (minutes) is characteristic of hyperacute rejection.
- **Systemic Symptoms:** Severe hypertension, dyspnea, DIC (petechiae, ecchymoses). These systemic signs are consistent with widespread vascular damage and complement activation.
- **Biopsy Findings:** Diffuse thrombosis of glomerular and peritubular capillaries, fibrin deposition, interstitial hemorrhage. These findings indicate acute vascular injury and coagulation abnormalities.
- **Immunofluorescence:** Strong IgM and C3 deposition along glomerular capillary endothelium. This confirms antibody-mediated injury and complement activation specifically targeting the graft endothelium.
- **Question Stem:** Asks for the *most direct* immunological mechanism responsible for the graft failure.

These clues collectively point towards a hyperacute rejection mediated by pre-formed antibodies against ABO antigens, leading to rapid complement activation, endothelial damage, thrombosis, and systemic complications.

##### 2. Immunological, Pathological & Virulence Deep-Dive (免疫机制、微生物致病机理与分子生物学深度剖析)
- **Hyperacute Rejection:** This is a form of transplant rejection occurring within minutes to hours after transplantation. It is mediated by pre-existing antibodies in the recipient against donor antigens, most commonly ABO blood group antigens or HLA antigens.
- **Mechanism:** Pre-formed IgG or IgM antibodies in the recipient bind to the corresponding antigens (e.g., B antigens in this case) on the endothelial cells of the transplanted organ. This antibody binding activates the classical complement pathway.
- **Complement Activation:** Activation leads to the formation of C3 convertase (C4b2a) and C5 convertase (C4b2a3b). C3 convertase cleaves C3 into C3a and C3b. C3b opsonizes the endothelial cells and further amplifies complement activation. C5 convertase cleaves C5 into C5a and C5b.
- **Endothelial Damage & Thrombosis:** C3a and C5a are potent anaphylatoxins, causing mast cell degranulation, histamine release, and increased vascular permeability. C5b initiates the formation of the membrane attack complex (MAC, C5b-9), which inserts into the endothelial cell membrane, causing cell lysis. C3b deposition also promotes platelet aggregation and activation of the coagulation cascade, leading to diffuse intravascular coagulation (DIC) and thrombosis within the graft microvasculature.
- **Clinical Manifestations:** The rapid endothelial damage, thrombosis, and complement activation lead to ischemia and necrosis of the graft, resulting in immediate graft failure. Systemic effects include hypotension, shock, DIC, and pulmonary hypertension.
- **Immunofluorescence:** Shows deposition of immunoglobulins (IgM, IgG) and complement components (C3, C4) along the endothelial surfaces of graft vessels.

In this specific case, the ABO incompatibility (A recipient, B donor) and the positive crossmatch confirm the presence of pre-formed anti-B antibodies. The immediate graft failure, systemic symptoms (hypertension, DIC), and biopsy findings (thrombosis, fibrin deposition, hemorrhage) with IgM and C3 deposition are classic features of hyperacute rejection mediated by anti-ABO antibodies.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A): T-cell mediated cytotoxicity is characteristic of acute cellular rejection, typically occurring days to weeks after transplantation. It involves cytotoxic T lymphocytes (CTLs) recognizing donor MHC class I molecules on graft cells, leading to cell lysis. This process is slower than hyperacute rejection and does not involve pre-formed antibodies or immediate thrombosis. The vignette describes immediate failure and complement deposition, not T-cell infiltration or cytotoxicity.
- (B): Antibody-dependent cell-mediated cytotoxicity (ADCC) involves antibodies coating target cells, which are then recognized and killed by effector cells like NK cells expressing Fc receptors. While antibodies are involved, ADCC is typically associated with acute or chronic rejection, not the immediate, complement-driven thrombosis seen in hyperacute rejection. Furthermore, the primary mechanism in hyperacute rejection is complement activation leading to endothelial damage and thrombosis, not direct cell killing by NK cells.
- (D): Immune complex-mediated rejection (Type III hypersensitivity) involves the formation of immune complexes between antibodies and soluble antigens, which deposit in tissues (like glomeruli in the graft) and activate complement, leading to inflammation and tissue damage. While complement is involved, this mechanism typically occurs later (days to weeks) and involves deposition of complexes in tissues, not primarily on the endothelium causing immediate thrombosis. The vignette describes antibody binding directly to endothelial antigens, not deposition of soluble complexes.
- (E): Direct activation of endothelial cells by donor leukocytes is not a recognized primary mechanism of transplant rejection. While leukocytes can contribute to inflammation and rejection, the immediate, antibody- and complement-mediated vascular damage described in the vignette is not caused by direct activation of recipient endothelial cells by donor leukocytes.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点与易混淆病原体/免疫缺陷横向对比):
| Feature                 | Hyperacute Rejection (Type II) | Acute Cellular Rejection (Type IV) | Acute Antibody-Mediated Rejection (Type II/III) | Chronic Rejection |
| :---------------------- | :----------------------------- | :-------------------------------- | :--------------------------------------------- | :---------------- |
| **Timing**              | Minutes to hours               | Days to weeks                     | Days to weeks                                  | Months to years   |
| **Mediator**            | Pre-formed Abs (IgG, IgM)      | T cells (CTLs, Th cells)          | Donor-specific Abs (DSA)                       | T cells, Abs      |
| **Target Antigen**      | ABO, HLA, Endothelium          | Donor MHC Class I/II              | HLA, Endothelium                               | Various           |
| **Mechanism**           | Complement activation, Thrombosis | Cell lysis, Inflammation          | Complement activation, ADCC, Inflammation       | Fibrosis, Vascular changes |
| **Vessel Changes**      | Thrombosis, Fibrin Deposition   | Endothelialitis, Infiltration     | Endothelialitis, Thrombosis                     | Glomerulopathy, Arteriosclerosis |
| **Immunofluorescence** | IgM, C3 on endothelium         | T cells in interstitium            | IgG, C4d on endothelium                         | Fibrosis, Inflammation |
| **Clinical Presentation**| Immediate graft failure, DIC    | Graft dysfunction, Fever           | Graft dysfunction, Fever, Hypertension          | Gradual graft failure |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
Hyperacute rejection is a rapid, antibody-mediated process caused by pre-formed antibodies against donor antigens, primarily ABO or HLA. It manifests within minutes to hours post-transplant with immediate graft failure and systemic signs like DIC due to complement activation and thrombosis. Differentiate it from acute cellular rejection (T-cell mediated, days-weeks) and acute antibody-mediated rejection (later onset, DSA-mediated). Key features are pre-existing antibodies, immediate onset, and vascular thrombosis with complement deposition.

<a id='question-139'></a>

### Question 139: Transplantation: Part 3 Focus 39 (Rejection)
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Understanding the cellular and molecular mechanisms of acute cellular rejection in organ transplantation, specifically the role of T lymphocytes (CD8+ cytotoxic T cells) and their effector mechanisms against allogeneic graft cells. (理解器官移植中急性细胞性排斥的细胞和分子机制，特别是T淋巴细胞（CD8+细胞毒性T细胞）的作用及其对异体移植排斥细胞的效应机制。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 52-year-old male with end-stage renal disease due to diabetic nephropathy receives a kidney transplant from a deceased donor with one HLA mismatch at the A locus. He is started on standard immunosuppression with tacrolimus, mycophenolate mofetil, and prednisone. Three weeks post-transplant, he develops fever, flank pain, decreased urine output, and elevated serum creatinine. A renal biopsy reveals diffuse interstitial inflammation with infiltration of mononuclear cells, tubular cell injury, and evidence of endothelialitis. Immunofluorescence staining shows deposition of C4d in peritubular capillaries. Flow cytometry analysis of the peripheral blood shows a significant increase in CD8+ T cells expressing granzyme B and perforin. Which of the following is the MOST likely mechanism by which the recipient's immune system is directly damaging the graft cells?

(A) Antibody-dependent cell-mediated cytotoxicity (ADCC) mediated by NK cells recognizing graft cells coated with donor-specific antibodies.
(B) Complement-mediated lysis of graft endothelial cells due to deposition of pre-formed anti-HLA antibodies.
(C) Direct cytotoxicity mediated by CD8+ T lymphocytes recognizing foreign MHC class I molecules on graft cells.
(D) Macrophage-mediated phagocytosis of graft cells opsonized by antibodies and complement.
(E) Neutrophil-mediated inflammation and tissue damage triggered by activation of the alternative complement pathway on graft surfaces.

#### Correct Answer: C

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical/Microbiological Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Profile:** 52-year-old male, end-stage renal disease (ESRD) from diabetic nephropathy, kidney transplant recipient. This sets the stage for transplant rejection.
- **Graft Type & Mismatch:** Kidney transplant from a deceased donor with one HLA mismatch (A locus). HLA mismatch is a major trigger for rejection.
- **Immunosuppression:** Standard regimen (tacrolimus, mycophenolate mofetil, prednisone). Despite immunosuppression, rejection can still occur.
- **Timeline:** 3 weeks post-transplant. This timeframe is typical for acute rejection, particularly acute cellular rejection (ACR).
- **Clinical Presentation:** Fever, flank pain, decreased urine output, elevated creatinine. These are classic signs of acute graft dysfunction, suggestive of rejection.
- **Biopsy Findings:** Diffuse interstitial inflammation, mononuclear cell infiltration, tubular cell injury, endothelialitis. These histological features are characteristic of ACR.
- **Immunofluorescence:** C4d deposition in peritubular capillaries. C4d is a marker of complement activation, often seen in antibody-mediated rejection (AMR), but can also be present in ACR, particularly in cases with mixed rejection or endothelial injury. However, the primary histological picture points towards cellular rejection.
- **Flow Cytometry:** Increased CD8+ T cells expressing granzyme B and perforin. This is a key finding. CD8+ T cells are cytotoxic T lymphocytes (CTLs), and granzyme B/perforin are effector molecules used by CTLs to kill target cells. This strongly implicates cell-mediated cytotoxicity.
- **Question Stem:** Asks for the MOST likely mechanism of direct graft cell damage.

##### 2. Immunological, Pathological & Virulence Deep-Dive (免疫机制、微生物致病机理与分子生物学深度剖析)
- **Acute Cellular Rejection (ACR):** Primarily mediated by T lymphocytes, specifically CD4+ helper T cells (Th1 phenotype) and CD8+ cytotoxic T lymphocytes (CTLs).
- **Mechanism of CD8+ T cell-mediated rejection:**
    - **Recognition:** CD8+ T cells recognize foreign MHC class I molecules (allo-MHC class I) presented on the surface of graft cells (e.g., tubular epithelial cells, endothelial cells) via their T cell receptor (TCR). The HLA mismatch (A locus in this case) provides the alloantigen.
    - **Activation:** TCR engagement, along with co-stimulatory signals (e.g., CD28-B7 interaction), leads to T cell activation.
    - **Effector Function:** Activated CD8+ CTLs kill target cells through two main mechanisms:
        1. **Perforin/Granzyme Pathway:** CTLs release perforin, which forms pores in the target cell membrane, and granzymes (serine proteases), which enter the target cell through these pores and activate caspases, leading to apoptosis.
        2. **Fas/FasL Pathway:** CTLs express Fas ligand (FasL) on their surface, which binds to Fas (CD95) receptors on target cells, triggering the extrinsic apoptotic pathway.
- **Role of CD4+ T cells:** CD4+ T cells recognize allo-MHC class II molecules and secrete cytokines (e.g., IFN-γ, TNF-α) that activate macrophages, promote inflammation, and enhance the cytotoxic activity of CD8+ T cells. They also contribute to endothelialitis.
- **C4d Deposition:** While often associated with AMR (where donor-specific antibodies bind to graft endothelium and activate complement), C4d can also be found in ACR, potentially due to complement activation secondary to inflammation or endothelial damage caused by T cells. However, the dominant mechanism suggested by the increased CD8+ T cells with granzyme B/perforin is direct cell-mediated cytotoxicity.
- **Distinguishing ACR from AMR:** ACR is characterized by T cell infiltration and cell-mediated damage, while AMR is characterized by antibody deposition, complement activation, and endothelial injury. The flow cytometry findings strongly favor ACR.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A) **Antibody-dependent cell-mediated cytotoxicity (ADCC) mediated by NK cells recognizing graft cells coated with donor-specific antibodies.** This describes a mechanism of AMR, where antibodies bind to graft cells, and NK cells (or other Fc receptor-bearing cells) recognize the Fc portion of the antibody and kill the target cell. While antibodies can be involved in rejection, the key finding here is the increase in cytotoxic CD8+ T cells with effector molecules, pointing away from ADCC as the *primary* mechanism described.
- (B) **Complement-mediated lysis of graft endothelial cells due to deposition of pre-formed anti-HLA antibodies.** This is another mechanism of AMR, specifically involving direct complement-mediated damage. Pre-formed antibodies (present before transplant) can bind to graft endothelium and activate the classical complement pathway, leading to cell lysis. While C4d is present, the dominant evidence (CD8+ T cells with granzyme B/perforin) points to cell-mediated cytotoxicity, not complement lysis as the primary mechanism.
- (D) **Macrophage-mediated phagocytosis of graft cells opsonized by antibodies and complement.** This is also a mechanism associated with AMR, where antibodies and complement components (like C3b) opsonize graft cells, making them targets for phagocytosis by macrophages. Again, the strong evidence for CD8+ T cell cytotoxicity makes this less likely as the *primary* mechanism.
- (E) **Neutrophil-mediated inflammation and tissue damage triggered by activation of the alternative complement pathway on graft surfaces.** Neutrophils are typically involved in acute inflammation and bacterial infections, not the primary mechanism of T cell-mediated graft rejection. While complement activation can occur, neutrophils are not the main effector cells in ACR.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点与易混淆病原体/免疫缺陷横向对比):
| Feature                 | Acute Cellular Rejection (ACR)                                  | Antibody-Mediated Rejection (AMR)                                  |
| :---------------------- | :------------------------------------------------------------- | :----------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **Primary Mediators**   | T lymphocytes (CD4+ Th1, CD8+ CTLs)                             | Antibodies (donor-specific HLA antibodies)                          |
| **Key Cells**           | CD8+ T cells, CD4+ T cells, Macrophages                       | B cells, NK cells, Macrophages, Neutrophils, Endothelial cells      |
| **Histology**           | Interstitial inflammation, mononuclear cell infiltrate, tubular injury, endothelialitis | Endothelialitis, C4d deposition, Glomerulitis, Arteritis, Capillaritis |
| **Mechanism**           | Direct CTL cytotoxicity (perforin/granzyme, Fas/FasL), Macrophage activation | ADCC, Complement-mediated lysis, Phagocytosis, Antibody-mediated inflammation |
| **Flow Cytometry**      | Increased CD8+ T cells (esp. with granzyme B/perforin)          | Increased B cells, potentially NK cells                           |
| **C4d Deposition**      | Can be present, but less specific than in AMR                    | Hallmark finding, especially in peritubular capillaries           |
| **Example Scenario**    | Patient with HLA mismatch, standard immunosuppression, biopsy shows T cell infiltrate | Patient with pre-formed antibodies, rapid graft dysfunction, C4d+ biopsy |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
Acute cellular rejection is primarily mediated by T lymphocytes, particularly CD8+ cytotoxic T cells, which directly kill graft cells via perforin/granzyme and Fas/FasL pathways. Differentiating ACR from AMR is crucial, relying on histological features (T cell infiltrate vs. endothelialitis/C4d), flow cytometry (CD8+ T cells vs. B cells/NK cells), and clinical context. Remember that C4d deposition can occur in both, but is more characteristic of AMR.

<a id='question-140'></a>

### Question 140: Transplantation: Part 3 Focus 40 (Occurs)
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Understanding the immunological mechanisms underlying graft rejection, specifically focusing on the role of T cells (CD8+ cytotoxic T lymphocytes) and their interaction with MHC class I molecules on graft cells, leading to direct allorecognition and graft damage. (理解移植物排斥的免疫机制，特别是T细胞（CD8+细胞毒性T淋巴细胞）的作用及其与移植物细胞MHC I类分子的相互作用，导致直接同基因识别和移植物损伤。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 52-year-old male with end-stage renal disease due to diabetic nephropathy receives a kidney transplant from a deceased donor with one HLA mismatch at the A locus. Post-transplant, he is maintained on immunosuppressive therapy including tacrolimus, mycophenolate mofetil, and prednisone. Six months after transplantation, his serum creatinine begins to rise, and a renal biopsy reveals interstitial inflammation with lymphocytic infiltrates and tubular atrophy. Immunofluorescence staining shows C4d deposition in peritubular capillaries, indicating antibody-mediated rejection (AMR). However, the patient's clinical course is characterized by a gradual decline in graft function over several months, without acute episodes of fever or severe rejection requiring high-dose steroids. Flow cytometry analysis of the biopsy tissue shows a significant increase in CD8+ T cells infiltrating the graft parenchyma, particularly targeting tubular epithelial cells. These CD8+ T cells express granzyme B and perforin, and their cytotoxic activity is significantly enhanced compared to peripheral blood CD8+ T cells. The patient's anti-HLA antibodies are negative. Which of the following mechanisms is MOST likely responsible for the observed graft injury in this patient?

(A) Antibody-dependent cell-mediated cytotoxicity (ADCC) mediated by NK cells recognizing antibody-coated graft cells.
(B) Complement-dependent cytotoxicity (CDC) mediated by anti-HLA antibodies binding to graft endothelial cells.
(C) Direct allorecognition by recipient CD8+ T cells recognizing donor MHC class I molecules on graft cells.
(D) Indirect allorecognition by recipient CD4+ T cells recognizing donor MHC class II molecules presented by recipient antigen-presenting cells.
(E) T-cell receptor (TCR) signaling inhibition by donor HLA-G molecules expressed on graft cells.

#### Correct Answer: C

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical/Microbiological Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Profile:** 52-year-old male, end-stage renal disease, kidney transplant recipient. This sets the context for graft rejection.
- **Immunosuppression:** Tacrolimus, mycophenolate mofetil, prednisone. Indicates an attempt to prevent rejection, but rejection is still occurring.
- **Timeline:** 6 months post-transplant. This timeframe is typical for chronic rejection or late acute rejection.
- **Clinical Findings:** Rising serum creatinine, renal biopsy showing interstitial inflammation, lymphocytic infiltrates, tubular atrophy. These are signs of graft dysfunction and injury.
- **Immunofluorescence:** C4d deposition in peritubular capillaries. This suggests antibody-mediated rejection (AMR), but the clinical course is gradual, and anti-HLA antibodies are negative. C4d deposition can sometimes be seen in T-cell mediated rejection (TCMR) as well, or represent a non-specific finding.
- **Flow Cytometry:** Increased CD8+ T cells infiltrating graft parenchyma (tubular epithelial cells), expressing granzyme B and perforin, enhanced cytotoxic activity. This strongly points towards a T-cell mediated process, specifically cytotoxic T lymphocytes (CTLs) directly attacking graft cells.
- **Negative Anti-HLA Antibodies:** Rules out classical AMR as the primary mechanism.
- **Question Stem:** Asks for the MOST likely mechanism responsible for the observed graft injury.

##### 2. Immunological, Pathological & Virulence Deep-Dive (免疫机制、微生物致病机理与分子生物学深度剖析)
- **Graft Rejection Types:**
    - **Hyperacute Rejection:** Occurs within minutes to hours, mediated by pre-formed anti-HLA antibodies (e.g., ABO incompatibility). Leads to complement activation, endothelial damage, thrombosis. Not consistent with the 6-month timeframe.
    - **Acute Rejection:** Occurs days to weeks post-transplant. Can be T-cell mediated (TCMR) or antibody-mediated (AMR). TCMR involves T cells recognizing donor MHC molecules (direct or indirect pathway). AMR involves anti-HLA antibodies binding to graft endothelium, activating complement and/or ADCC. The vignette describes features suggestive of both (C4d, lymphocytic infiltrate), but the negative anti-HLA antibodies and prominent CD8+ CTL infiltration point strongly towards TCMR.
    - **Chronic Rejection:** Occurs months to years post-transplant. Characterized by fibrosis, vascular changes (graft arteriosclerosis), and gradual loss of graft function. Often involves both cellular and humoral immunity.
- **T-Cell Mediated Rejection (TCMR):**
    - **Direct Pathway:** Recipient T cells (CD4+ and CD8+) directly recognize intact donor MHC molecules (MHC class I and II) on graft cells. This requires minimal processing and presentation by recipient APCs. CD8+ T cells recognize donor MHC class I on graft cells, leading to direct killing via perforin/granzyme release or Fas/FasL interaction. CD4+ T cells recognize donor MHC class II, leading to cytokine production and activation of other immune cells. This pathway is considered the primary mechanism for acute cellular rejection. The vignette's finding of CD8+ T cells infiltrating tubular epithelial cells and expressing cytotoxic molecules strongly supports this mechanism.
    - **Indirect Pathway:** Recipient APCs process donor MHC molecules (from graft cell death or shedding) and present peptides derived from these molecules on their own MHC class II molecules to recipient CD4+ T cells. These CD4+ T cells then activate other immune cells, including CD8+ T cells. This pathway is thought to be more important in chronic rejection and rejection of vascularized grafts.
- **Antibody-Mediated Rejection (AMR):** Mediated by anti-HLA antibodies (or antibodies against non-HLA antigens). Mechanisms include:
    - **Complement-Dependent Cytotoxicity (CDC):** Antibodies bind to graft endothelial cells, activating the classical complement pathway, leading to cell lysis.
    - **Antibody-Dependent Cell-Mediated Cytotoxicity (ADCC):** Antibodies bind to graft cells, and NK cells (or other Fc receptor-bearing cells) recognize the Fc portion of the antibody, leading to target cell lysis.
    - **Inflammation and Endothelial Injury:** Antibody binding can also cause inflammation and endothelial cell activation, leading to thrombosis and ischemia.
- **The Vignette's Specifics:** The gradual decline, negative anti-HLA antibodies, and prominent CD8+ T cell infiltration with cytotoxic activity targeting graft parenchyma (tubular cells) are classic features of T-cell mediated rejection, specifically the direct pathway involving CD8+ CTLs recognizing donor MHC class I. The C4d deposition might be a non-specific finding or related to minor complement activation during the T-cell mediated process, but the negative anti-HLA antibodies make classical AMR (CDC, ADCC) less likely as the primary driver. HLA-G is an inhibitory molecule, not involved in rejection.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- **(A) Antibody-dependent cell-mediated cytotoxicity (ADCC) mediated by NK cells recognizing antibody-coated graft cells.** This is a mechanism of AMR. The vignette explicitly states that the patient's anti-HLA antibodies are negative, making this mechanism unlikely to be the primary cause. ADCC requires antibodies.
- **(B) Complement-dependent cytotoxicity (CDC) mediated by anti-HLA antibodies binding to graft endothelial cells.** This is another mechanism of AMR. Again, the negative anti-HLA antibodies make this mechanism unlikely. CDC primarily targets endothelial cells, while the vignette describes CD8+ T cells targeting tubular epithelial cells.
- **(D) Indirect allorecognition by recipient CD4+ T cells recognizing donor MHC class II molecules presented by recipient antigen-presenting cells.** While indirect allorecognition occurs, the vignette highlights the prominent infiltration of CD8+ T cells directly targeting graft cells (tubular epithelial cells) and expressing cytotoxic molecules. Direct allorecognition by CD8+ T cells is the more direct and potent mechanism described in the findings. Indirect recognition primarily involves CD4+ T cells initially.
- **(E) T-cell receptor (TCR) signaling inhibition by donor HLA-G molecules expressed on graft cells.** HLA-G is a non-classical MHC class I molecule that can inhibit T cell responses. It is not typically associated with causing graft rejection; rather, it's studied for its potential immunomodulatory and immunosuppressive roles in transplantation. It does not explain the observed cytotoxic T cell infiltration and graft injury.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点与易混淆病原体/免疫缺陷横向对比):
| Feature                     | Direct Allorecognition (TCMR) | Indirect Allorecognition (TCMR) | AMR (ADCC/CDC) | HLA-G Role          |
| :-------------------------- | :----------------------------- | :------------------------------ | :------------- | :------------------ |
| **Primary Cells Involved**  | CD8+ T cells (CTLs), CD4+ T cells | CD4+ T cells                    | Antibodies, NK cells | T cells (inhibitory) |
| **Target Antigen**          | Intact Donor MHC I/II          | Donor MHC peptides on Recipient MHC II | Graft Endothelium | T cells            |
| **Mechanism**               | Direct CTL killing             | CD4+ T cell activation -> CTL activation | ADCC, CDC      | Immunosuppression  |
| **Antibodies Required?**    | No                             | No                              | Yes            | No                 |
| **Typical Timeframe**       | Acute                          | Chronic, Vascularized Grafts     | Acute/Chronic  | N/A                |
| **Vignette Fit**            | **Excellent**                  | Less likely (CD8+ focus)        | Poor (No Abs)   | Poor               |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
The primary mechanism of acute T-cell mediated graft rejection involves direct allorecognition by recipient T cells, particularly CD8+ cytotoxic T lymphocytes (CTLs), recognizing donor MHC class I molecules on graft cells, leading to direct cell killing. Differentiating between T-cell mediated rejection (TCMR) and antibody-mediated rejection (AMR) is crucial, especially when findings overlap (e.g., C4d deposition). Key differentiators include the presence/absence of anti-HLA antibodies and the primary effector cells involved (T cells vs. antibodies/NK cells).

<a id='question-141'></a>

### Question 141: Transplantation: Part 3 Focus 41 (within)
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Understanding the immunological mechanisms underlying graft rejection, specifically the role of T cells (CD4+ and CD8+) and antibodies in acute cellular rejection and antibody-mediated rejection (AMR), and differentiating between these processes based on clinical presentation and histopathology. (理解移植物排斥的免疫机制，特别是T细胞（CD4+和CD8+）和抗体在急性细胞排斥和抗体介导的排斥（AMR）中的作用，并根据临床表现和组织病理学区分这些过程。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 52-year-old male with end-stage renal disease due to diabetic nephropathy receives a kidney transplant from a deceased donor. His pre-transplant panel reactive antibody (PRA) screen was negative. He is maintained on standard immunosuppression consisting of tacrolimus, mycophenolate mofetil, and prednisone. Three weeks post-transplant, he develops fever, flank pain, decreased urine output, and elevated serum creatinine (from a baseline of 1.2 mg/dL to 4.5 mg/dL). A renal biopsy is performed. Histological examination reveals diffuse interstitial inflammation with lymphocytic infiltrates, tubular epithelial cell injury, and focal endothelialitis. Immunofluorescence microscopy shows C4d deposition in peritubular capillaries. Flow cytometry analysis of the peripheral blood shows a significant increase in CD4+ T cells expressing HLA-DR, and a moderate increase in CD8+ T cells. Serum analysis reveals elevated levels of donor-specific antibodies (DSA) against HLA class I antigens.

Which of the following immunological processes is MOST likely responsible for this patient's acute graft dysfunction?

(A) Acute antibody-mediated rejection (AMR) characterized primarily by C4d-negative interstitial fibrosis and tubular atrophy.
(B) Acute cellular rejection (ACR) characterized primarily by C4d-positive glomerulitis and endothelialitis.
(C) Chronic antibody-mediated rejection (CAMR) characterized primarily by C4d-negative transplant glomerulopathy.
(D) Acute cellular rejection (ACR) characterized primarily by C4d-negative interstitial inflammation and tubular injury.
(E) Acute antibody-mediated rejection (AMR) characterized primarily by C4d-positive glomerulitis and endothelialitis.

#### Correct Answer: (D)

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical/Microbiological Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Profile:** 52-year-old male, end-stage renal disease (diabetic nephropathy), kidney transplant recipient. This sets the stage for potential graft rejection.
- **Immunosuppression:** Tacrolimus, mycophenolate mofetil, prednisone. Standard regimen, but rejection can still occur.
- **Timeline:** 3 weeks post-transplant. This timeframe is typical for acute rejection episodes (both cellular and antibody-mediated).
- **Symptoms:** Fever, flank pain, decreased urine output, elevated creatinine. Classic signs of acute graft dysfunction/rejection.
- **Biopsy Findings:**
    - **Histology:** Diffuse interstitial inflammation with lymphocytic infiltrates, tubular epithelial cell injury, focal endothelialitis. These findings are characteristic of acute rejection, involving both cellular (lymphocytic infiltrates, tubular injury) and potentially antibody-mediated (endothelialitis) components.
    - **Immunofluorescence:** C4d deposition in peritubular capillaries. C4d is a stable breakdown product of the classical complement pathway activation. Its presence in peritubular capillaries is a strong indicator of antibody-mediated injury, often associated with AMR. However, C4d can also be seen in ACR, particularly in the setting of T-cell mediated endothelial damage.
    - **Flow Cytometry:** Increased CD4+ T cells expressing HLA-DR, increased CD8+ T cells. This indicates T-cell activation, a hallmark of ACR. HLA-DR expression on CD4+ T cells signifies their activation status.
    - **Serum Analysis:** Elevated donor-specific antibodies (DSA) against HLA class I antigens. This confirms the presence of antibodies targeting the graft, strongly suggesting an antibody-mediated component.
- **Question Stem:** Asks for the MOST likely immunological process responsible for the acute graft dysfunction.

##### 2. Immunological, Pathological & Virulence Deep-Dive (免疫机制、微生物致病机理与分子生物学深度剖析)
- **Acute Cellular Rejection (ACR):** Primarily mediated by T lymphocytes (CD4+ helper T cells and CD8+ cytotoxic T cells) recognizing donor HLA antigens. Histopathology shows interstitial inflammation (lymphocytes, macrophages, eosinophils), tubular injury, and sometimes endothelialitis. C4d deposition is typically absent or minimal in pure ACR, although it can be present in some cases, especially if endothelialitis is prominent. T-cell activation markers like HLA-DR expression on CD4+ cells are increased.
- **Acute Antibody-Mediated Rejection (AMR):** Mediated by pre-formed or de novo donor-specific antibodies (DSAs) binding to donor HLA antigens on endothelial cells, activating complement and leading to endothelial injury, inflammation, and thrombosis. Histopathology shows microvascular inflammation (endothelialitis), C4d deposition in peritubular capillaries (a key feature, though not universally present or specific), and sometimes glomerulitis. DSAs are present in the serum. T-cell activation can also occur secondary to AMR.
- **Chronic Antibody-Mediated Rejection (CAMR):** Occurs later (months to years post-transplant). Characterized by transplant glomerulopathy (double contours of the glomerular basement membrane), peritubular capillary basement membrane thickening, and interstitial fibrosis/tubular atrophy. C4d deposition may be present but is often less prominent or patchy compared to AMR. DSAs are typically present.
- **Chronic Cellular Rejection:** Characterized by interstitial fibrosis, tubular atrophy, and vascular changes (glomerulosclerosis, transplant vasculopathy). T-cell infiltrates are less prominent than in ACR.
- **Distinguishing ACR vs. AMR:** The presence of DSAs and C4d deposition strongly suggests AMR. However, C4d can be seen in ACR, and DSAs can be present without causing rejection (sensitization). The combination of histological features (lymphocytic infiltrates, tubular injury, endothelialitis), C4d deposition, DSA presence, and T-cell activation markers helps differentiate. In this case, the presence of DSAs, C4d deposition, endothelialitis, and T-cell activation points towards a mixed picture, but the question asks for the *most likely* process given the *combination* of findings. The presence of C4d deposition and DSAs are hallmarks of AMR. While T-cell activation is present (suggesting ACR), the C4d and DSA findings are more specific for AMR. The histological description (lymphocytic infiltrates, tubular injury, endothelialitis) can be seen in both, but the C4d deposition strongly favors AMR. The question asks for the *most likely* process, and the combination of DSA and C4d deposition is highly suggestive of AMR. However, the histological findings described (lymphocytic infiltrates, tubular injury) are more classically associated with ACR. The key is the C4d deposition and DSA presence. The question asks for the *most likely* process. Given the presence of DSAs and C4d deposition, AMR is strongly implicated. The histological findings are consistent with both ACR and AMR. The presence of DSAs and C4d deposition are stronger indicators of AMR than the histological findings alone are of ACR. Therefore, AMR is the most likely process. Let's re-evaluate the options based on the typical features.

##### 3. Re-evaluation based on options and typical features:
- The vignette describes features of both ACR (lymphocytic infiltrates, tubular injury, T-cell activation) and AMR (DSA, C4d deposition, endothelialitis).
- Option (A) describes AMR but incorrectly states it's C4d-negative.
- Option (B) describes ACR but incorrectly states it's C4d-positive.
- Option (C) describes CAMR, which is chronic, not acute, and typically C4d-negative or patchy.
- Option (D) describes ACR and correctly states it's typically C4d-negative. This aligns well with the lymphocytic infiltrates and tubular injury, and T-cell activation.
- Option (E) describes AMR and correctly states it's typically C4d-positive. This aligns well with the DSA and C4d deposition, and endothelialitis.

- **Resolution:** The presence of DSAs and C4d deposition are strong indicators of AMR. The histological findings (lymphocytic infiltrates, tubular injury, endothelialitis) are consistent with both ACR and AMR. However, the combination of DSA and C4d deposition is considered diagnostic for AMR according to the Banff criteria. While ACR can sometimes have C4d deposition, it's not a defining feature. Given the presence of both DSA and C4d deposition, AMR is the most likely diagnosis, even though ACR features are also present. The question asks for the *most likely* process. The presence of DSAs and C4d deposition are key features pointing towards AMR. Option (E) correctly identifies AMR and its association with C4d deposition. Option (D) correctly identifies ACR and its association with C4d negativity, but the presence of DSAs and C4d deposition makes ACR less likely as the *primary* process, although it might be occurring concurrently or be a consequence of the AMR. The most specific markers for AMR (DSA, C4d) are present. Therefore, AMR is the most likely process.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A): Incorrect. AMR is characterized by C4d deposition in peritubular capillaries, not absence. The vignette explicitly states C4d deposition.
- (B): Incorrect. ACR is typically C4d-negative. The vignette explicitly states C4d deposition. While histological features are consistent with ACR, the C4d positivity points away from pure ACR.
- (C): Incorrect. CAMR is a chronic process, occurring months to years post-transplant, characterized by transplant glomerulopathy and fibrosis. The vignette describes an acute process (3 weeks post-transplant) with acute histological findings.
- (D): Incorrect. While the histological findings (lymphocytic infiltrates, tubular injury) and T-cell activation are consistent with ACR, the presence of DSAs and C4d deposition strongly suggests AMR. ACR is typically C4d-negative.
- (E): Correct. This option correctly identifies AMR and its characteristic feature of C4d deposition. The presence of DSAs further supports AMR. The histological findings, while overlapping with ACR, are also consistent with AMR (especially endothelialitis). The combination of DSA, C4d deposition, and histological features makes AMR the most likely diagnosis.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点与易混淆病原体/免疫缺陷横向对比):
| Feature             | Acute Cellular Rejection (ACR) | Acute Antibody-Mediated Rejection (AMR) | Chronic Antibody-Mediated Rejection (CAMR) |
|----------------------|---------------------------------|------------------------------------------|-------------------------------------------|
| **Timing**           | Days to weeks post-transplant    | Days to weeks post-transplant             | Months to years post-transplant            |
| **Mediator**         | T cells (CD4+, CD8+)             | Donor-Specific Antibodies (DSAs)          | DSAs                                      |
| **Histology**        | Interstitial inflammation, tubular injury, endothelialitis (sometimes) | Endothelialitis, C4d deposition, glomerulitis (sometimes) | Transplant glomerulopathy, fibrosis, tubular atrophy |
| **C4d Deposition**   | Typically negative               | Typically positive in peritubular capillaries | Variable, often patchy or negative        |
| **DSA Presence**     | Absent                           | Present                                  | Present                                   |
| **T-cell Activation**| Present (HLA-DR+)                | Can be present secondary to inflammation | Less prominent                           |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
Acute graft rejection can be cellular (ACR) or antibody-mediated (AMR). Key distinguishing features include the presence of DSAs and C4d deposition (AMR) versus T-cell mediated inflammation without significant C4d (ACR). Histology can overlap, but endothelialitis and C4d deposition are hallmarks of AMR. Remember that chronic rejection (CAMR) occurs later and has distinct histological features like transplant glomerulopathy. Differentiating ACR from AMR is crucial for appropriate management.

<a id='question-142'></a>

### Question 142: Transplantation: Part 3 Focus 42 (minutes)
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Understanding the immunological mechanisms underlying acute cellular rejection in solid organ transplantation, specifically the role of T lymphocytes (CD8+ cytotoxic T cells) and their recognition of alloantigens presented by MHC class I molecules on graft cells. (理解实体器官移植中急性细胞性排斥的免疫机制，特别是T淋巴细胞（CD8+细胞毒性T细胞）的作用及其对移植细胞表面MHC I类分子呈递的异体抗原的识别。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 52-year-old male with end-stage renal disease due to diabetic nephropathy receives a kidney transplant from a deceased donor with two HLA mismatches at the A and B loci. Post-transplant, he is maintained on a standard immunosuppressive regimen including tacrolimus, mycophenolate mofetil, and prednisone. Approximately 10 days after transplantation, he develops fever, flank pain, decreased urine output, and elevated serum creatinine. A renal biopsy reveals interstitial edema, inflammatory cell infiltrates primarily composed of lymphocytes, and evidence of tubular injury. Immunofluorescence staining shows C4d deposition in peritubular capillaries. Flow cytometry analysis of the biopsy sample identifies a significant increase in CD8+ T cells infiltrating the graft tubules and interstitium, many of which are expressing Fas ligand (FasL). The patient's serum contains antibodies against donor HLA antigens.

Which of the following cellular mechanisms is MOST directly responsible for the observed tubular injury in this patient?

(A) Antibody-dependent cell-mediated cytotoxicity (ADCC) mediated by NK cells recognizing antibody-coated graft cells.
(B) Complement-dependent cytotoxicity (CDC) mediated by antibodies binding to donor HLA antigens on graft cells.
(C) Direct cytotoxicity mediated by CD8+ T lymphocytes recognizing donor HLA class I antigens presented on graft cells.
(D) Macrophage-mediated inflammation triggered by antibody-complement complexes deposited in the glomeruli.
(E) Neutrophil-mediated inflammation triggered by the activation of the alternative complement pathway on graft endothelial cells.

#### Correct Answer: C

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical/Microbiological Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Profile:** 52-year-old male, end-stage renal disease (ESRD) due to diabetic nephropathy, kidney transplant recipient. This sets the stage for transplant rejection.
- **Transplant Details:** Deceased donor, 2 HLA mismatches (A and B loci). HLA mismatches are a major risk factor for rejection.
- **Immunosuppression:** Tacrolimus (calcineurin inhibitor), mycophenolate mofetil (purine synthesis inhibitor), prednisone (corticosteroid). Standard regimen, but rejection can still occur.
- **Timeline:** ~10 days post-transplant. This timeframe is typical for acute rejection, particularly T-cell mediated rejection.
- **Symptoms:** Fever, flank pain, decreased urine output, elevated creatinine. Classic signs of acute kidney graft dysfunction.
- **Biopsy Findings:** Interstitial edema, lymphocytic infiltrates (predominantly lymphocytes), tubular injury. Suggests acute rejection.
- **Immunofluorescence:** C4d deposition in peritubular capillaries. Indicates activation of the classical complement pathway, often associated with antibody-mediated rejection (AMR), but can also be seen in T-cell mediated rejection (TCMR) with antibody involvement or overlap syndromes.
- **Flow Cytometry:** Increased CD8+ T cells infiltrating tubules/interstitium, expressing FasL. This is a key finding pointing towards direct cytotoxic T-cell mediated damage. CD8+ T cells are the primary effectors in acute cellular rejection targeting MHC class I. FasL expressed by CD8+ T cells binds to Fas on target cells (graft cells), inducing apoptosis.
- **Serum Antibodies:** Presence of donor HLA antibodies. Suggests potential antibody-mediated component, but the dominant cellular infiltrate and FasL expression point towards a significant T-cell mediated component.
- **Question Stem:** Asks for the MOST direct mechanism of tubular injury.

##### 2. Immunological, Pathological & Virulence Deep-Dive (免疫机制、微生物致病机理与分子生物学深度剖析)
- **Acute Cellular Rejection (ACR):** Primarily mediated by T lymphocytes recognizing donor HLA antigens as foreign.
- **T-cell Recognition:**
    - **CD4+ T cells (Helper T cells):** Recognize donor MHC class II molecules on antigen-presenting cells (APCs) within the graft. They release cytokines (e.g., IFN-γ, TNF-α) that activate macrophages and recruit other inflammatory cells, contributing to graft damage.
    - **CD8+ T cells (Cytotoxic T Lymphocytes - CTLs):** Recognize donor MHC class I molecules expressed on virtually all nucleated cells of the graft (including tubular epithelial cells). This recognition leads to direct killing of the graft cells.
- **CD8+ T cell Cytotoxicity:** CTLs kill target cells through several mechanisms:
    - **Perforin/Granzyme Pathway:** CTLs release perforin (forms pores in target cell membrane) and granzymes (enter through pores, activate caspases, inducing apoptosis).
    - **Fas/FasL Pathway:** CTLs express Fas ligand (FasL), which binds to Fas (CD95) receptors on target cells, triggering the extrinsic apoptosis pathway. This is highlighted in the vignette.
- **MHC Class I Presentation:** MHC class I molecules present endogenous antigens (peptides derived from proteins synthesized within the cell) to CD8+ T cells. In transplantation, donor MHC class I molecules present donor peptides, which are recognized as foreign by the recipient's CD8+ T cells.
- **Antibody-Mediated Rejection (AMR):** Mediated by antibodies (often pre-formed or de novo) against donor HLA antigens. Antibodies can activate complement (CDC) or mediate cell killing via ADCC (NK cells, macrophages) or opsonization. C4d deposition is a marker of complement activation, often seen in AMR.
- **Overlap Syndromes:** Many rejection episodes involve both cellular and humoral (antibody-mediated) components.
- **In this case:** The prominent infiltration of CD8+ T cells expressing FasL directly targeting graft tubules strongly indicates that direct cytotoxicity by CD8+ T cells is a major mechanism of tubular injury. While C4d deposition and donor HLA antibodies suggest an antibody component, the specific cellular findings point most directly to CD8+ T cell-mediated killing.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A) **Antibody-dependent cell-mediated cytotoxicity (ADCC) mediated by NK cells recognizing antibody-coated graft cells.** ADCC is a mechanism of AMR. While antibodies are present and C4d is deposited, the primary cellular infiltrate identified is CD8+ T cells expressing FasL, directly targeting tubules. ADCC involves NK cells or macrophages binding to Fc receptors on antibody-coated cells, which is not the mechanism highlighted by the CD8+ T cell findings.
- (B) **Complement-dependent cytotoxicity (CDC) mediated by antibodies binding to donor HLA antigens on graft cells.** CDC is another mechanism of AMR. Antibodies bind to donor HLA antigens, activating the classical complement pathway, leading to the formation of the membrane attack complex (MAC) and cell lysis. While C4d deposition suggests complement activation, the vignette emphasizes the direct cytotoxic action of CD8+ T cells (FasL expression) on tubules, not MAC-mediated lysis.
- (D) **Macrophage-mediated inflammation triggered by antibody-complement complexes deposited in the glomeruli.** Macrophages are involved in both ACR (activated by CD4+ T cells) and AMR (phagocytosing immune complexes or antibody-coated cells). However, the primary site of injury described is the tubules, and the key effector cells identified are CD8+ T cells expressing FasL, not macrophages triggered by glomerular immune complexes.
- (E) **Neutrophil-mediated inflammation triggered by the activation of the alternative complement pathway on graft endothelial cells.** Neutrophils are typically associated with acute bacterial infections or certain types of vasculitis, not the primary mechanism of acute cellular rejection. The alternative complement pathway is less commonly implicated in transplant rejection compared to the classical pathway (activated by antibodies). The key findings point to T-cell mediated cytotoxicity, not neutrophil-mediated inflammation.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点与易混淆病原体/免疫缺陷横向对比):
| Feature                 | Acute Cellular Rejection (ACR) - T-cell mediated | Antibody-Mediated Rejection (AMR) |
|-------------------------|---------------------------------------------------|------------------------------------|
| **Primary Effector Cells** | CD8+ T cells (CTLs), CD4+ T cells                 | Antibodies, Complement, NK cells, Macrophages |
| **Target Antigens**       | Donor MHC Class I (CD8+), Donor MHC Class II (CD4+) | Donor HLA antigens (MHC I & II)   |
| **Mechanism of Injury**   | Direct cytotoxicity (Perforin/Granzyme, FasL), Cytokine release | CDC, ADCC, Opsonization, Inflammation |
| **Key Histopathology**    | Lymphocytic infiltrates (esp. CD8+), Tubular injury | C4d deposition, Endothelialitis, Glomerulitis, Neutrophil infiltration |
| **Key Markers**           | CD8+ T cells, FasL expression                     | Donor HLA antibodies, C4d          |
| **Typical Timing**        | Days to weeks post-transplant                      | Days to months post-transplant      |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
Acute cellular rejection is primarily mediated by T lymphocytes, particularly CD8+ cytotoxic T cells, which directly kill graft cells expressing foreign MHC class I molecules. The Fas/FasL pathway is a key mechanism of this direct cytotoxicity. Differentiating ACR from AMR is crucial, as treatments differ. Key features of ACR include T-cell infiltrates and direct cell killing, while AMR involves antibodies, complement activation (C4d deposition), and effector cells like NK cells and macrophages.
